He felt something warm begin to seep through his clothes and hair, and his vision blurred
from all of the concussions that he likely had.

For an agonizing amount of time, Akira was forced to grit and endure the barrage. Arms
buckling, willpower waning.

And yet, never breaking.

It wasn't until the final ball of the onslaught struck that his desperate defence fell, and he
collapsed onto the trio that he had just protected.

"Shit! Kurusu!? Kurusu!" Ryuji cried. He scrambled to get out of the pile and immediately
assessed his fellow teen's situation. And it was bad. Really bad. Like his body was
literally smoking bad.

Akira was insanely banged up. His entire body was peppered with severe impact marks.
From the shine and deformations on his back and pants, he probably had some friction burns



as well

What was also noticeable was the blood that soaked through his clothes, staining his jacket in
pants in dark red liquid. And with his head... Christ, it was bleeding like crazy. There was so
much that it was pooling around his head, soaking into the carpet and staining his glasses.

Ryuji was joined by a tearful Ann and a horrified Morgana, gathering around Akira's body.
They were all sickened at how grievously wounded he was. And it was all to protect them?

Morgana had seen the indentations and cracks on the floor where the balls had missed. To
fracture and warp them like that...what was the damage like on Akira?

Dammit! He couldn't even summon his Persona for first aid without all of these knights
closing in on them!

"Kurusu!? Can you hear me?!" Ryuji asked desperately. He received no reply.

"W-We've gotta get him to a hospital!" Ann cried. The colour had almost completely drained
from her face, and seeing those injuries had her almost throwing up. For a moment, she had
even doubted if Akira was alive, if not for the subtle rise and fall of his chest. Each shuddery
exhale sounded painful.

Of course, over their panicked cries, there came that infuriating laughter once more.

"Hahahahahahahaha! Holy crap! This is brilliant! I knew that you'd come out of that
fucked up, but you took so many of my spikes willingly! There is not a single word in the
world that can define how ungodly stupid you are!" The king announced in hysterics.

And then, to make matters worse, the entirety of the knights began to laugh at them too with
haunting, distorted voices. Pointing at them, clapping their hands, all with those
expressionless masks. It was like something straight out of a nightmare.

"To think, not only did I have to ravage my beautiful floor, but you now stain it with your
broken body and unworthy blood..." He turned to a knight. "Make arrangements to have
those fixed at once."

"At oNcE, mY KiNg!"

Kamoshida turned back and waved his hand for silence. The sight of Akira's injuries made
his foul smile spread. He stepped closer, his voice dripping with sadistic glee.

"Looks like you were all talk in the end weren't you, Kurusu?" Kamoshida taunted. "You
thought you could defy me? You thought you could stand against my power? And now,
you're broken, just like all the rest!"

Akira could hardly hear the insult. His entire body was swimming in sheer agony, and it
made it hard to focus on anything. His vision had dissolved into nothing but blurry blobs, and
everything he heard sounded like it was underwater.



He could feel his blood run down his face. He could feel his skin burn and his head throb. He
was definitely in the worst shape he'd ever been.

And yet, his arms strained, his teeth gnashed and weak gasps left his lips. He had to get up.
He had to! If he didn't...

But he couldn't. His strength had all but left him. The power that had carried him through so
many tribulations just wasn't able to answer the call. All he could do was lie there, in a pool
of his own stupid blood.

But there was one thing that he wasn't able to ignore, no matter how messed up he was, and it
was Kamoshida's taunts and jeers. That he could only listen to them made him feel weaker
than ever. And yet, something bugged him beyond everything else...

How did Kamoshida know his name?

Akira knew that he had never said it around him? So how did Kamoshida know it?

"Eh, in any case. It'd be no fun torturing someone who's already broken, so I've got no more
desire to keep you alive." Kamoshida huffed as he held out his hand, where another
volleyball was placed, to the horror of the group.

"Perhaps this spike will turn you into paste, or maybe it'll make your organs shoot out of
your mouth, or just maybe, it'll drill a hole straight through you. However it happens, this
is my killshot!"

Kamoshida reared his hand back, the rubber of the ball creaking under his grip.

It was a time of despair for the group. Kamoshida held all the cards. He had taken down their
strongest player, and none of the others were particularly strong to begin with.

And yet, Ryuji stood up, glaring at Kamoshida like he would all the world's evils. He blocked
the way of the ball, both nerves and courage flooding his veins.

Akira, who had protected them without any hesitation...what kind of man would he be if he
couldn't do the same?

"You're not touching him!" He snarled at Kamoshida.

The king scoffed. "What? You think I won't shoot through you, Sakamoto? If anything, I'll
shoot harder. Maybe I'll aim for your other leg; finish what I started. A second case of self-
defence', the school will call it." He taunted.

Ryuji flinched, but he still stood his ground. He clenched his fists in anger, his eyes full of
hatred. "Shut up, you bastard! We won't let you get away with this!"

"You stupid brat. You should know by now that I always get away with this." Kamoshida
sneered. "Tell the teachers, your parents, even the police. Those vultures are so desperate
to ride my coattails that they'll let me get away with anything!"



"What...!?" Ann whispered. The police of all people ignored actual reports made on
Kamoshida? But he was just a gym teacher. Surely he didn't have that kind of pull!

"You nearly killed a transfer student who has nothing to do with you 'cuz he stood up to you.
You can't just pretend that didn't happen!" Ryuji shouted at Kamoshida.

The king chuckled and gestured to Akira's broken body on the ground.

"What? Him? His body could be found in a dumpster and nobody would care. In fact,
they'd probably rejoice at the fact that the 'troublesome transfer student' is no longer a
problem."

"Troublesome?" Ryuji asked.

"Kurusu isn't the noble person that he's led you to believe. I looked into his records before
he came over here." Kamoshida explained, leaning on the balcony. "He didn't come here
for a change of scenery or to challenge himself. He's here on probation, for assaulting a
man."

Ryuji, Ann and even Akira gasped, the latter quite painfully.

"Of course, I did my civic duty and made sure that everybody at school knows of the
dangerous student, but what a shame, all my efforts are for naught since he will be dying
now."

"You...l-leaked my record!?" Akira grunted in disbelief. But he hadn't even officially attended
school yet. This guy was that much of a scumbag that he decided to just screw up any chance
of Akira having a normal high school life before he had even shown up?

"Wait, you mean...you actually assaulted someone...?" Ann asked Akira. She sounded so
confused, and Akira didn't blame her.

"False...accu-accusation." Akira hissed. His voice was barely above a whisper. Not that he
wanted to be insensitive, but he'd prefer to have this conversation when he wasn't a mangled
mess.

"You mean...he was a dick?" Ann replied.

"The dickiest..." Akira groaned. He couldn't help but give a pained smile at Ann's summation.
"Though he's a close contender..." He tried to glare at Kamsoshida, but he couldn't even move
his head. A sudden surge of pain shot through his body and Akira gasped. His vision was
gradually getting blurrier, and the longer that he lay there, wallowing in agony, the less he
could hear. Voices and sounds were getting distant. He felt a soft hand grasping his own
while two small limbs gently gripped his head and chin.

Morgana really was quite furry.

"Still, this may be your only chance. That criminal is still breathing. If you leave him
behind, I might just give you some time to escape." Kamoshida told the blonde.



"H-Huh!? What are you sayin'!?" Ryuji asked in complete alarm.

"What? You're so stupid that you need me to spell even that out? I'm gonna do to him as
what I do to all those I deem criminals." To emphasise his point, he held up the
ball. "Either you give me his life for a chance at yours, or don't, and you all die
together." Kamoshida tossed the ball up in the air and caught it over and over, each rubbery
thunk echoing across the room.

"L...Leave Kurusu behind? There's no way we can do that! Right?!" Ann looked to Morgana
hopefully. And yet, much to her horror, she saw the cat's round head lowered and his face
noticeably conflicted.

He wasn't actually considering that...?!

"Morgana...we can't...!" Ann whispered.

"Lady Ann..." Morgana squeaked. He looked to the girl, and then to Akira. The man who had
so recklessly, yet bravely defended them from Kamoshida's assault. The man who had freed
him when neither Ann nor Ryuji could decide.

But the time for debts was not now.

Kamoshida may have been a bastard, but offering them a head start was actually sort of
generous. At the cost of Akira's life though...

"I definitely know a way out of here, and I have my trump card set. But I can't...we can't take
him with us. We need that time. There are knights everywhere!" Morgana told Ann
desperately. "I...I know that he saved our lives, but we don't have a choice. The only thing we
can do is...make sure he doesn't die for nothing." He told her solemnly.

"No..." Ann whimpered.

"Come on!" Kamoshida's voice rang out. "It's not like this is a hard choice. He's just a
criminal with no future. Worthless. Scum-of-the-earth. A blight!" Kamoshida spat.

"You're right...it isn't a hard choice at all," Ryuji muttered. The boy took a deep breath and
then stepped forward. "I barely know the guy, but all he's done, ever since we met him, is pull
our asses out of the fire!" He said, with an increasing voice.

"You say he's a criminal, but he's been nothin' but heroic. He stopped Takamaki from getting
into your car, he saved me from your knights, and he freed Monagana!"

"It's...! Mngh..." Morgana wanted to correct him, but he quickly realised that it would be
ignored. That, and the surge of guilt that he felt when Ryuji declared that it had been Akira
who freed him caused the cat to let the mispronunciation slide.

"I don't care if he did assault someone! I don't care that he's got a record or that he's on
probation. He's a good person, and he's also really cool! But you...!"

Ryuji took another step towards Kamoshida.



"You took away his chance of a normal high school life before you even knew what he
looked like! You're forcing Takamaki into something she doesn't want, and you treat
everyone else like shit!"

"Sakamoto..." Ann gasped. She couldn't tear her eyes away from the blond.

He spat on the ground, before the king.

"So, thanks but no thanks, I'm not going to abandon anyone! Take your "mercy" and shove it
up your ass!"

"Vermin!" Kamoshida cursed. "You reject the benevolence of your king?! I'll take all your
limbs, and then your head!"

"Shut up! You're no king! You're just a third-rate gym teacher! And I'm sick and tired of you
looking down on me with that stupid smile on your face! KAMOSHIDA!"

"You made me wait quite a while."

And then Ryuji's eyes flashed gold...

What exactly happened from that moment, Akira couldn't really tell. He heard a lot of faint
screaming, and then a blinding blue light. Then there was the sound of metal being thrown
around. Every pained breath that he took was now filled with something...weird, something
that he had difficulty placing. Like steam from a bath, but instead of a sensation of moisture,
it felt more tingly, like he was breathing in...electricity?

"...N...ay! Bl...air...ona...?!"

"...Per...at's...ona...?"

"...o...ti...we...ve...ance...!"

He could hear Ann and Morgana talking, but he couldn't quite make out what they were
saying. They sounded far away, even though he knew that they were right next to them.

"...ome...orro!"

Through his closed eyelids, he noticed a second blast of blue light, way closer to him than the
first, and he heard a feminine scream, presumably Ann's. What the hell was going on!? Try as
he might, he wasn't able to open his eyes and find out. He was trapped in darkness with
nothing but pain as his constant company.

"...ia!"

At that moment, Akira felt a subtle shift in his body. The excruciating pain that had engulfed
him was slowly ebbing away, replaced by a peculiar warmth. It started as a faint flicker, like a
tiny flame dancing against his skin, but started growing stronger, spreading through his body



It almost felt like when he flooded his body with Heat, but it was noticeably different. But it
didn't seem to be invasive or nefarious. It was a tingling touch that worked its way into every
fiber of his being, and to his amazement, was healing his wounds.

Those craters left in his back and head healed at an astounding rate. His hearing got clearer,
and little by little, the weakness latching onto him was almost peeled away.

The darkness that shrouded Akira's vision soon retreated, and little by little, he managed to
force his eyes open. He saw Ann clearly, her mouth covered while her sapphire eyes
portrayed nothing but relief. "K-Kurusu! You're okay!" She cried.

She didn't sound like she was underwater right now, so that was good.

"I'm...okay?" He parroted. How was he okay? He wasn't exactly complaining, but he got
completely messed up. How had he recovered from wounds like those?

"I've done all I can. You were a light breeze away from my spell not being able to do
anything." Morgana told him.

"Spell?" Akira asked, looking over to Morgana. "What, you're talking about magic or som-
HOLY SHIT!?"

Despite everything weird that he had seen today, he couldn't stop himself from yelling in
alarm, because if this wasn't a concussion making him see things, then there was a massive
figure made out of fire with triangle-like proportions and tiny legs hovering behind Morgana
with a rapier at the ready!

Like, what the hell!?

"Who...are you?" Akira asked whatever the hell this thing was. He was pretty sure that it
wasn't human.

"Hah! This is Zorro, my Persona! Cool, isn't he?" Morgana stated proudly.

Akira didn't know whether or not he should agree. He didn't even know what he was looking
at. "Persona?" He asked.

"Yup! Zorro is my rebellious will made manifest! I used his power to heal your wounds." He
explained.

"Yeah, he said "Dia" or something like it, and then you were covered in green sparkles," Ann
told Akira. "Although..." She turned her head to Morgana, looking a little miffed. "Why
didn't you just do that when he first got hurt? I really thought that he was gonna die!" She
hissed.

"I-It was a tense situation. I th-thought that if I summoned him prematurely, then all the
shadows might attack us. I can't take on those many knights..." Morgana replied. It didn't
seem to have any fire of defence to it though, probably because that criticism came from his
precious Lady Ann.



"I-I don't get it..." Akira grunted, as he finally managed to push himself up to his knees. It
was still painful, but far from the mincemeat he was before. "If you couldn't summon...Zorro
before because of the knight situation, then what changed?" He questioned.

His answer came in the form of Ann pointing a finger behind him.

Akira grunted as he turned himself around. He was just in time to see a knight crash to the
floor. It wouldn't have been all that unusual to see that, except for the fact that
he wasn't responsible this time.

The culprit himself was quite unexpected.

Clad in black, a familiar figure was going to town on those knights. His yellow-gloved hands
gripped a basic pipe as a weapon, an improvision that Akira was intimately familiar with.

Wrapped around his knees were protective metal kneepads, and clinging to his back was what
Akira could only describe as a spine of metal. It looked punkish, but also kind of cool.

But there was one thing, that Akira was drawn to. One thing that their saviour wore that
caused something inside him to stir.

His red ascot.

...

...

Nah, it was the grey mask that sat upon his face. It looked like the upper half of a skull.
Determined brown eyes stared out from behind it as he clobbered a knight across the head
with the pipe.

And then there was that bright, blond hair that immediately indicated who this warrior was.

"Is that... Sakamoto?" Akira asked in total confusion, because the last time he was fully
conscious, he was pretty sure that Ryuji wasn't wearing an outfit like that.

"Yeah..." Morgana muttered in reply. "He's awakened to a Persona."

"Awakened to a Persona?" Akira repeated, his mind racing with questions. "What does that
even mean? Are you saying...he's like you now?"

"It means that he's unlocked a part of himself. His true self. An unquenchable desire to stand
against authority, and rip off his own mask of conformity." Morgana explained. "It's hard to
put it into words, really." He added, still watching Ryuji with awe.

The boy gave a sinister grin as he dodged a swing from a knight's sword and gripped the edge
of his mask. A wave of power suddenly flooded the room and with a geyser of blue fire,
Ryuji's mask was removed.

"Rock on, Captain Kidd! Let's knock a few more of these guys around!"



Akira had to cover his eyes from the ensuing flash. It stung to look at, but when it faded,
what hovered behind Ryuji was undoubtedly similar to Morgana's own Persona, in feel that
is. Their appearances themselves could not have been any more different. While Morgana's
Persona seemed to have some sort of refined air, worthy of a renowned swordsman, Ryuji's
one was more...wild in nature.

His avatar of rebellion came in the form of a skeletal pirate clad in an outfit made for
swashbuckling. A pirate hat emblazoned with the Jolly Roger sat upon its head. And in place
of a stereotypical hook hand was a cannon.

And while that was all going in, the pirate was standing on a miniature black pirate ship with
a menacing face painted on its stern. Akira couldn't help but wonder if the pirate was big, or
the boat small...

Either way, both Ryuji and his Persona were kicking some serious ass. Not only was he
knocking them down with his pipe, but summoning his Persona brought down a volley
of...lightning?

There was no other way about it. One after the other, Ryuji was summoning genuine bolts of
lightning, utterly massacring his foes. Another thing that Akira had to add to the list of
supernatural bullshit that he had seen today.

At least it explained the electricity in the air.

"And that's why he's wearing... that?" he asked, gesturing to Ryuji's outfit.

"It's a manifestation of his rebellious will," Morgana chimed in. "Those clothes are like an
armor to protect people from being affected by this world. Kamoshida doesn't have any
authority over Blondie anymore and now has to resort to brute force to get him to submit."

"Well, whatever Sakamoto is doing, it's working," Ann said, gesturing to the pile of defeated
knights around them. She raised her fist in the air triumphantly "Go Sakamoto!"

"It's not that simple, Lady Ann! When you first awaken to your Persona, you can unleash
power close to your maximum potential with it. The trade-off though, is that it's super taxing
on your body. If Ryuji goes much farther, he's gonna burn out!" Morgana revealed, his eyes
wide in panic.

Those eyes were soon shared by Ann and Akira, as they looked over to Ryuji, still
slaughtering knights with his pipe and lightning.

"Can...Do you think that he can take out all of those knights on his own? It looks like he has
taken down almost half of them." Ann asked, wringing her hands nervously.

"Don't worry about that," Akira grunted, trying to push himself to his feet. "Any that he can't
take out, I will." He declared.

"You're hurt!" Ann reminded Akira.

"And you don't have a Persona! A regular person can't fight shadows without a Persona."



"I can," Akira replied.

"H-Huh? What do you mean?" Morgana questioned.

"Well, he isn't wrong, when Sakamoto and I were captured, he had to beat up a bunch of
knights to get to us." Ann recounted.

"What...?! But that's not...there's just no way that...!" Morgana was totally stunned by the
news of Akira's actions but quickly regained his focus.

"Ah, whatever! Listen, Frizzy Hair, if you can fight, then you can run, right?" Morgana
asked. "Get Blondie over here, we need to escape!" The urgency in the cat's voice was
unmistakable. At this moment, they could all escape, but they had to leave now!

"Sakamoto!" Akira called, stumbling to his feet.

Ryuji took a swing at another knight before he turned towards them. There was an excited
glint in his eyes from behind the skull-like mask "Hey guys!" He cried. He gestured to
himself. "I don't know what happened, but I feel like such a badass now! I even got this
awesome outfit. Admit it, you gotta be jelly!" He announced.

Then he realised that Akira was looking much better, and his excited expression turned to
surprise "Kurusu, you're okay, dude?" he called out in relief.

"Yeah! Morgana fixed me up! Listen, Sakamoto, we gotta get the hell out of here, while we
still can! He told the boy.

"What!? But I'm just gettin'-"

"Ryuji! Now!" Akira repeated firmly, he barely noticed that he used his first name.

"Urk! F-Fine, I'm coming!" Ryuji replied. He then turned around and pointed to the irate king
about them. "This isn't over, Kamoshida! Not by a long shot!"

The king snarled at Ryuji. The gall of that slimeball. To awaken to such a power to match the
majority of his servants. What on earth had happened?

Well, no matter. He had a backup plan.

"No. It isn't." He grunted, before snapping his fingers. The group's attention was quickly
drawn to the remaining guards, thinking that they were about to try something.

Akira's misaligned instincts kicked in too late to stop what happened next.

"Kyaaaaa!"

As it turned out, they were looking in the wrong place.

"Takamaki!"



"Lady Ann!"

It had happened in a split second. An unknown entity had somehow made its way behind
them and with speed that made it look like a blur, it snatched up Ann. By the time that Akira
was reaching out, the blonde had already been taken.

Taken over to Kamoshida.

The assailant with Ann in tow came to a stop right next to the man, giving everyone else the
chance to look at it.

Or rather, her.

"What the...!?"

"I c-can't be seein' this right..."

"Holy crap! It's two meow-velous and beautiful girls!"

Ann was restrained in a full nelson, but the arms holding her own were dainty, and not
muscular in any way. That was because the one holding Ann in place was...

Ann.

Another Ann who looked identical to the original in every way but her fashion sense was
completely risqué. This Ann was "covered" in only a black and pink bikini and wore a pair of
similarly coloured cat ears on her head. It was weird, uncomfortable and definitely not
something that a high schooler should wear. It looked like this Takamaki worked at the
sleaziest cabaret clubs!

"Wh-What...? Why the 'eff are there two Takamakis!?" Ryuji cried in total confusion. This
place had given them way too many questions already, but a clone of Ann in one of the
skimpiest outfits that they had ever seen really took the cake.

"It's gotta be the Palace Ruler's cognition of Lady Ann. That's how he sees her~" Morgana
explained, with his eyes still overtaken by hearts.

And wasn't that another keyword here...? "Cognition," "Palace," "Shadows," Morgana had
been spouting all of these terms ever since they had met him, but he hadn't clarified on any of
them. Granted, they didn't have a lot of time, but that didn't make any of them less curious.

"Let her go!" Ryuji demanded raising a...

Was...was that a goddamn shotgun!?

Where the hell had he gotten that?

"I ain't asking again, asshole! Let Takamaki go!" Ryuji warned, with his finger curling around
the trigger of the weapon.



"Oh please! Do you think I'm scared of that? Even you should know that a shotgun relies
more on power than accuracy. Which of those two do you think is more important right
now?" Kamoshida replied with a mocking tone. He seemed to have found his sickening
smile once things had turned back in his favour.

Ryuji snarled. He knew that what Kamoshida was saying was true. It would be impossible to
accurately aim at Kamoshida from this distance without Ann possibly getting caught in the
spread. The same went for his new lightning powers.

But like, what else could he do? Kamoshida had taken Ann hostage with some weird and
lewd-as-hell version of herself. What could he do?

"Hey," Ryuji turned to Morgana. "Does your Persona give you a ranged weapon?

Morgana had thankfully snapped out of his trance due to the seriousness of the situation but
proceeded to look embarrassed.

"I-I have a slingshot..."

"For real, man?" Ryuji cried in exasperation. A slingshot was next to useless in this situation.

"I-It's more accurate than your stupid shotgun!" Morgana snapped back.

"Don't think I don't see you peasants conspiring over there. Rest assured, if any of you try
anything, I'll break her goddamn neck!" Kamoshida warned.

Ann, who was understandably freaking out about the fact that she was not only in
Kamoshida's clutches but also being held in place by her doppelganger in the most mortifying
attire that she had ever seen, looked to the king in horror.

"Wh-What...!?" She shrieked.

Kamoshida gave her a side glance polluted by his ugly smile."What, you think you're too
important for me to kill? That spike barrage wasn't meant for you as well? Tough shit,
Takamaki, because I don't need someone who constantly refuses me...not when I have your
ideal replacement right here~" Kamoshida taunted. He reached out and slowly ran his hands
through the alternate Ann's hair. Disturbingly, that made the girl purr in pleasure.

"Mmmn~ Like, anybody who rejects King Kamoshida's love should tooooooootally be
executed. Snap their neck~ Cut out their tongue~ Drawn and quartered~" The duplicate
sang in possibly the most bimbo-ish, airheaded tone that anyone here had heard. The fact that
it was said with a distorted version of her own voice only added to Ann's terror.

"Well said, my sexy little kitten. Very well said~" Kamoshida responded.

And with no shame whatsoever, the man grasped the alternate Ann's chin, as one would a
lover, turned her head to meet her gaze and brought his head down to lock lips with her. Like,
hard locking lips. As in full-on, frenching each other, despite the fact that one was over
double the other's age.



"Ugh! Dude, that's so 'effing messed up! Stop!" Ryuji demanded. Watching Ann kissing
Kamoshida, regardless of whether it was the real one or not, was revolting!

Ann couldn't control her panicked breathing as she heard the sound of the two making out
only inches behind her. Her pupils dilated, her eyes flowed with tears, and the urge to vomit
surged in her throat. In a frenzy, she pulled and screamed, yet her clone's grip didn't budge.

The helplessness of the situation was palpable. Ryuji was holding his shotgun aloft for no
other reason than false hope that he would be able to get a clear shot.

Akira had even less chance of making it there before Kamoshida could do anything. He
would have to battle through all those knights at once, and Kamoshida could kill Ann a
hundred times over before he got there. Morgana would have the same problem.

"Dammit, we can't just do nothing!" Ryuji cursed. It was a notion that Akira agreed with, but
there wasn't any feasible path out of this. Not when someone's life was on the line. What
could they do? He could see that Ryuji's arms were starting to shake. For as staunch as he
was being, he was starting to wear out, exactly as Morgana described.

Kamoshida chuckled and pulled away from the kiss with the alternate Ann. "What? You
jealous, Sakamoto? I'm not surprised. Women would never be attracted to a punk ass like
yourself. Could be raining pussy and you'd get hit in the head with a dick!"

"Sh-Shut up!" Ryuji screamed. "Make your freaky Takamaki sex doll let go or I'll..."

"You'll what? I hold all the cards. I can kill her any time I want, and all you can do is just
watch like a bunch of pissants. Maybe I'll fulfil what her slutty body has been destined for
before I rip her to pieces..."

Kamoshida began to pace around the practically catatonic Ann. "Remove your clothes..." He
played with the hem of Ann's blazer.

"Get you raring to go..." He dragged his hand across her thigh.

"And then, when I'm done playing with you, I'll kill you in front of all of your little
friends!" He grabbed Ann's pigtail and gave it a harsh pull.

"YOU FUCKING SICKO!" Ryuji screamed at Kamoshida.

"HAHAHAHAHAHAHAHAHAHA!" The maniacal laughter of the king rang out across the
room.

Akira's heart pounded in his chest as he watched the horrifying scene unfold before him. The
despair that gripped him seemed suffocating, his mind racing with the realization of just how
hopeless the situation had become.

Kamoshida held all the power, toying with Ann's life and taunting them with sadistic glee.
The words he uttered, filled with cruelty and malice, cut through Akira like a knife.



As he glanced at Morgana, he saw the despair mirrored in the cat's eyes. All he could feel
was anger and frustration. They were trapped, trapped in Kamoshida's sadistic game with no
clear way to save Ann.

Akira's gaze shifted back to the girl, her eyes glazed over in a state of shock and fear. The
sight struck him deeply, a painful reminder of the consequences of their failure. He couldn't
bear to witness the degradation Ann was subjected to, the threat of her imminent death
hanging in the air. The weight of it all pressed down on Akira, draining his spirit and leaving
him on the precipice of despair.

At that moment, the walls seemed to close in on him, the world narrowing down to complete
darkness. Hopelessness engulfed Akira. Acting, thinking; he couldn't do either.

Despite all of his strength, there was nothing that he could do to stop this. What was the point
of his abilities if he couldn't use them where they mattered most?

Ann was terrified. Ryuji was exhausted. Morgana wasn't strong enough.

He was useless. He had nothing but his gut. He was up against a wall...

 

And yet somewhere deep down, there was a spark of determination that refused to be snuffed.

 

He had to find a way. He had to turn this impossible situation around.

He had to...

HE HAD TO!

And that was when Akira saw a familiar blue.

Glinting in the darkness of his despair, a glowing orb of calm blue gently washed over him.
From that light came a curious creature that fluttered around in the dark.

"A...butterfly?" Akira muttered in confusion.

Its ethereal blue wings flapped to and fro, eventually circling around his head. At that
moment, Akira heard a serene voice fill his ears.

"This is truly an unjust game... Your chances of winning are almost none." It spoke. "But if
my voice is reaching you, there may yet be a possibility open to you..."

An unjust game?

A possibility open to him?

What did that mean?



To his surprise, the butterfly circled him one last time before settling atop his frizzy black
hair. It opened and closed its wings silently, having designated this spot as its perch.

At that moment, Akira heard footsteps. The clicking of shoes coming towards him. The
sound resonated around him, and he couldn't deny how unnerving it felt. Like he was being
stalked or hunted. There was only one light in this place, and it rested atop Akira's head.

The footfalls grew closer and closer, and Akira found his heart racing...

And then they stopped. The last footstep echoed to the ends of the dark plain. The one behind
them stood just out of range of the light, and that made it even creepier.

For a moment, Akira considered whether he should step forward or not. Meet this guy head-
on.

And then he heard a voice that stole his breath away.

"Akira..."

The voice was deep, yet carried a sense of calm and warmth that Akira instantly recognized.
It was a voice that had guided him through many hardships, a voice that had imparted
wisdom and strength in his darkest moments.

 

From the abyss stepped out Kazuma Kiryu.

 

"U-Uncle Kaz?" he whispered, his voice choked with emotion.

His caretaker stepped forward, an ordinary motion, yet seemed so full of power.

The man grew closer and Akira found himself frozen in confusion. How was this happening?
How was Kiryu here?

Any attempt to ask the question only resulted in incoherent mumbling.

"You're in trouble, aren't you?" Kiryu asked. He stared right into Akira's eyes like he was
looking straight into his soul. His eyes were filled with understanding and patience, as if he
already knew every burden resting upon Akira's heart.

Akira was silent and only nodded his head in response.

Kiryu's gaze softened, and he reached out to place a reassuring hand on Akira's shoulder.

"It's hard. The feeling of being backed into a corner. The feeling of having no options left. It's
frustrating. Scary too." Kiryu spoke softly. "I know all too well how it feels, to face down the
impossible."



He continued staring into Akira's eyes with not a shred of hesitance.

"But I fought anyway. I fought because I didn't have a choice. Because the people I loved
were in danger. Because some scumbag believed that he deserved to stand above everyone
and everything. And you Akira...I have no doubt that you can do the same."

Akira hung his head for a moment, feeling ashamed of his despair. But Kiryu's words gave
him a sense of hope, a flicker of strength that he thought he had lost. He looked up to Kiryu,
his eyes shining with gratitude.

"And...Wh-What if I'm not strong enough? Every time I tried to help someone, things have
just gotten worse..." Akira told him, disheartened.

"You will be," Kiryu stated firmly. "That dirty kid who showed up at Morning Glory one day
has grown into a wonderful, kind and strong young man... Someone who knows that helping
people is never wrong." Kiryu pressed his hand deeper into Akira's shoulder. "And that young
man, I believe, can do anything."

Akira was silenced by such a revelation. Kiryu believed in him that much?

"Uncle Kaz...what can we do? How can we get out of this? How can I save everyone? Can
you help me?"

Kiryu's face became serious. "I wish I could...but you're somewhere that I can't reach right
now. This battle, you'll have to fight on your own." Kiryu answered with a voice of regret.

"But that changes nothing." He declared. "You need to stop thinking that you're helpless. You
have the power to change the game, whatever it is. Find that power. Find that way to win. I
know you can do that, Akira Kurusu!"

Akira's breath hitched at the passion within Kiryu's voice. It was startling, yet riling at the
same time.

"Akira..." Kiryu's voice dropped to something wistful. "Many times in my life, I've been too
late to act, and bad things happened because of it. I don't want that to happen to you." Staring
at him seriously, he imparted one more fragment of wisdom.

"Never hesitate, got it?"

"Y-Yes! I got it, Uncle Kaz!" Akira responded stiffly.

Kiryu gave Akira a firm smile and then let him go. "Akira. I don't know where you are. But I
know that I'll find you, and bring you home..." Kiryu told Akira, slowly stepping back into
the darkness.

"Br-Bring me home...but Uncle Kaz, I can't-"

But the man had already vanished. Gone without a trace.



And Akira was left standing there, alone in the darkness once again with only the butterfly
keeping him company now.

He didn't know what just happened, he didn't know why he had just seen Kiryu in some kind
of weird vision.

And yet, that didn't matter. Uncle Kaz believed in him. He believed!

Akira would not do the man a disservice. He would see to it that his faith wasn't misplaced.

And that was when the pain began.

Agony like nothing he had felt before shot trough his mind. A terrible sensation that felt like
a red hot bar was being inserted into his brain. Even that barrage paled in comparison

"A-AAAAAAAAAAAAAAGHHH! RAAAAAAAAAGH!

It was so bad. He felt himself scream his throat raw, yet that didn't help the pain at all. He
lurched around like a crazy person, dislodging the butterfly atop his head and causing it to
flutter back to who knew where.

"Have you decided to stop pitying yourself?"

Akira could have sworn that he heard a voice, but he couldn't be sure, mostly because his
pain had doubled as the voice spoke. He didn't even think that it was possible for it to get any
worse.

"Grounded though you may be, you still have your fangs and your claws. Will you simply
dawdle until you lose them too?"

Akira didn't have much capacity in the way of thinking, but losing more than he had lost
already... That was unacceptable.

"N-No." He whispered between screams. "I can't lose anymore. I won't!"

"Very well... I have heeded your resolve." The voice replied.

The pain was now so bad that Akira had fallen to his knees, desperately cradling his head in
his hands.

"Vow to me. I am thou, thou art I. Thou who art refuse to let the weak suffer; to stay true to
your justice, and to never waver."

Akira could suddenly feel a weight on his face.

"Should thou be condemned to hold up the sky, or be chained to hell itself, our contract will
remain, everlasting!

Akira felt around his face. There was some kind of...mask in place. He was struck with the
uncontrollable urge to take it off, but when he tugged on it, it didn't move. Desperately, he



pulled and pulled. He heard the sound of tearing and screamed at the feeling of his skin
giving way. Blood ran down his face, but he didn't stop pulling.

"For too long you have wallowed in the shadows of legends that have been and legends to
be... Now, the time has come to forge a legend of thy own!"

RIIIIIP!

"AAAAAAAAAAAH!"

It was the guttural, agonised screaming that broke the standoff within the hall.

Everyone looked to Akira, who had fallen to his knees and was trying to get some kind of
mask off his face.

Ryuji recognised the scene immediately. After all, he had gone through the exact same thing
only moments ago. While he didn't feel that pain anymore, the memory of how excruciating
it was would stick with him for a long time...

Morgana watched the young man tug so hard on the mask seemingly fused to his face, that
blood started to run down it.

Was his own awakening that painful? He didn't know...

"I can't believe it. I thought it was weird that Blondie was the only one with the potential of
Persona. To think that he had it too..." He whispered to himself.

Ann, who had given up all hope at this point, saw Akira's face as it twisted in agony, saw his
subtle relief as the mask was torn off, and watched as a pillar of blue fire engulfed him, just
like what happened to Ryuji. Light slowly started to return to her eyes.

Could this be...?

Kamoshida's eyes widened as he watched. Whatever was happening seemed to be the same
thing that Sakamoto went through, and it had given him the power to take on his knights.

If this peasant, that could already fight his knights, woke to the same power...?

He still had a hostage, but he shouldn't take any chances. He had to stop this!

"Quickly! Kill him before he can try anything!" Kamoshida bellowed to his order of
knights. Several rushed out, swords drawn before the inexplicable tower of blue fire.

"Oh no you don't!" Ryuji turned and tried to rush to Akira's aid. "Tried" being the key word.
With a yelp, Ryuji felt the remainder of his strength leave him and he fell flat on his face. He
had reached his limit. "D-Dammit!"

Morgana was a bit fresher.



"No! Leave him alone!" Morgana warned. In a second, he had already summoned Zorro.

"Zorro, show your might!"

The Persona slashed its sword in the shape of a 'Z' and a gale of cutting winds rushed forth.

Unfortunately, each gust was small in size and was only able to injure a knight, instead of
outright defeating it. He got lucky and downed a couple, but that wasn't good enough. The cat
tried desperately to repel the knights, but they inevitably broke through and rushed towards
the pyre of flames, letting out battle cries as they prepared to slaughter the teenager cocooned
within.

Eight knights in total charged into that conflagration...and not even a second went by before
their cries were crudely silenced.

There was no heat to those flames, and yet those knights were incinerated, turned to ash by
what was less of a tower of blue flames and more of a protective wreath, keeping everything
out until the transformation had run its course.

The blue fire then started to shape. It's countless embers twisting into something thinner,
more humanoid...

Something that stared out from the azure pyre with malicious eyes the colour of molten
metal.

It didn't look anything like Ryuji's Captain Kidd, nor Morgana's Zorro. The blue fire traced
the form of something that looked far more demonic. Long, curved horns, giant, weathered
wings and an expression that belonged to what could only be a devil.

The room was filled with a peal of eerie laughter that sent chills down everyone's spines.

Under some misconception that he still had the situation under control, Kamoshida gripped
Ann's face threateningly. The twintailed girl gasped and tried to pull away, but both her clone
and Kamoshida held her still.

"I-I know you're in there, you piece of shit! Don't forget that I still have the bitch right
here" He spat. "You do anything and she's fucking dead! You hear me!? I'll kill her! I'll
kill-"

No one in the room could explain what happened next.

The flames vanished.

There was the sound of a gunshot, and the fake Ann fell away from the real one, a single
bullet hole in her head.

Akira was gone.

And Kamoshida felt something grip his arm.



Kamoshida grunted in pain and was forced to let go of Ann's face. He tried to pull his arm
away, but his aggressor's grip was ironclad. Who would dare-?

"No..."

At the sound of that familiar, aggravating voice, Kamoshida slowly started to turn his head.

It couldn't be... It wasn't possible!

He was suddenly met with the horrifying and cacophonous sound of metal clunking and
shrieking.

His knights. All of them...

Chests torn open, heads missing, run through with their own swords, raining down from
heights that they could never reach on their own.

They had all been defeated...no. Massacred…

His head turned a little more, his mind caught in a rising terror.

And eventually, his gaze was met, not by Takamaki, but by a pair of furious, grey eyes behind
a black and white domino mask.

"...You won't."

Chapter End Notes

I feel like this chapter was a little low quality. You should probably understand that I'm
going into this with the expectation that any readers will have knowledge of both
franchises which would allow me to gloss over certain situations. I wanted to be detailed
enough so that it doesn't seem lazy but not too detailed that it becomes redundant. I don't
wish to be a guy that submits an entire chapter based solely on dungeon crawling but I
feel that in this case, it's necessary for worldbuilding.



Chapter 4: Metaverse 101
Chapter Summary

I beat up a king, a cat is my teacher, and I learned about the Metaverse instead of
pythagoras theorem.

Chapter Notes
See the end of the chapter for notes

Swish...

 

Swish...

 

Swish...

The serene waves rolling upon the shore of the Okinawan beach had been his alarm clock for
several years now. To watch them wash against the borders of their little slice of home always
filled him with peace

And yet, all Kiryu Kazuma wished to do now was stay in bed.

The morning sun seeped through the curtains, casting a soft glow into his bedroom. Damn, he
should have closed the curtains last night...

The goateed man's eyes slowly opened, and he rubbed the sleep from them, his arm weighted
with sleeplessness.

With a groan, he shifted his body upwards. He had always been somewhat of a light sleeper,
but lately, his nights had become increasingly haunted by worry and confusion. All for a
simple reason.

He glanced at the calendar. 4/11

 

Three weeks since Akira had disappeared.

 

Three weeks they had failed to find him.



 

Three weeks of the void left by his absence growing with each passing day.

 

Morning Glory, one of the best things that had ever happened to him, had become sombre,
quiet. The absence of the orphanage's big brother figure was taking its toll on everyone. One
day he was there, and the next, he wasn't.

That night when Akira had failed to come home was the start of everything. Worry spread
through the dinner table like a virus, and it wasn't long before everyone realised that
something was wrong.

A day of searching the city turned up nothing for all of them. They searched arcades and
restaurants, pretty much any common teenage haunts, and came back with nothing. At the
end of the day, there was no doubt that Akira had gone missing.

Initially, Kiryu believed that there was no reason for Akira to suddenly drop off the face of
Okinawa, with no clues left behind and no leads to follow.

That was until, in a pooling of their findings, Riona had informed him that on the day of his
disappearance, she spotted Akira sitting outside Kiryu's room, eavesdropping. But if that was
true, the only thing that Akira could've possibly overheard before he disappeared was...

 

His conversation with Ayako...

 

The conversation where Ayako mentioned that Akira didn't belong at Morning Glory...

 

Shit.

 

Assuming that he ran off halfway through the conversation, he probably had the impression
that Ayako hated him, and yet, that was not true. The end of that conversation had the young
girl committed to making amends with Akira; to open up to him. A part that Akira didn't stick
around for, evidently.

Nobody could be blamed for thinking that Akira had just run away, heartbroken that his place
in Morning Glory was far from what he believed. Yet Kiryu didn't entertain that notion for
long. Akira was way too mature to do something so childish.

He couldn't say the same for Ayako though. The poor girl had been inconsolable when she
found out. She would hardly come out of her room nowadays, and anytime she did, she



looked miserable. She had said all of those mean things and now Akira was gone. She
believed that it was her fault that everyone's big brother was gone, no matter how much
Kiryu told her otherwise.

He couldn't let that lie. He wouldn't let his children agonize over something like that, just like
he wouldn't let Akira slip away from a home that he so desperately needed.

His first breakthrough was when he went to Akira's current high school on a hunch and was
informed that the teen had been expelled, due to a criminal record and arrested. That had
immediately set off alarm bells in his head. Expelled? Criminal record? How!?

Eagerly following the first real lead that he had, Kiryu had dropped by the Okinawa Police
Station and immediately questioned the reception, asking for Akira by name. But what he got
for his troubles was no less troubling. That being a shake of their heads and some legal jargon
that said they couldn't divulge incident details with unaffiliated parties.

Unaffiliated parties? He was Akira's goddamn caretaker! He was the first one that should
have been called when Akira had been arrested!

And yet, even though he definitely remembered registering Akira as a resident of Morning
Glory, he had been told that the youth he described had been living alone while his parents
were abroad. Of course, that was completely untrue, but the fact remained that somehow, in
some way, Akira's records had been altered.

Questions upon questions layered themselves in Kiryu's head and he had no solid answers for
any of them. He thought about pressuring the police to learn something, but that would just
get himself and Morning Glory in...

 

...in a lot of trouble...

 

Had Akira somehow changed his own records in an attempt to protect Morning Glory from
the consequences of his crime? And if he had, then how serious was the crime?

To be perfectly honest, a lawsuit aimed at Morning Glory would likely be ruinous for them.
They weren't exactly far from being in the red, and Kiryu admittedly didn't do too great a job
in keeping that a secret.

In recent months, it had gotten so bad that some of the children in Morning Glory were
considering finding some form of employment to support the orphanage, instead of attending
school and pursuing their passions. If Akira had placed the burdens of that incident on the
shoulders of Morning Glory, it would undoubtedly be their downfall, and he would never be
able to forgive himself.

So Akira had managed to take those burdens, the lawsuit and the ensuing stigma, and run
away with them, keeping them from harming Morning Glory.



Of course that didn't sit right with Kiryu. One of his children had been forced to make a cruel
decision to keep his family safe. No one to save him. Fighting a battle with no one to lend
their aid.

And that led Kiryu to his current dilemma. If he pressed any official sources to try and track
down the teen, then whatever Akira had done would go down the drain, and Morning Glory
would come under fire.

He couldn't just let Akira suffer in god knows where, and yet, all any further digging would
do was put them in jeopardy. What Akira had done, how he'd done it and where he was now,
he had no way of knowing any of that.

 

Should we even try to dig up the truth when Akira had tried so hard to bury it?

 

With no viable solutions, Kiryu had endured several restless nights, with no answer having
come to him.

Although come to think of it, he did drift off to sleep rather easily last night, but that in turn
came with a strange dream.

A vivid memory played in his mind's eye: wandering through an abyss of darkness, following
the light of a mesmerizing blue butterfly.

In that ethereal realm, he had caught a glimpse of Akira, his face etched with distress, his
body battered and bruised. And he was wearing glasses. Why was he wearing glasses?

They had exchanged words, their conversation a phantom echo in Kiryu's memory, elusive
and intangible. It was after making a promise to the teen, to find him no matter what, that
Akira faded away. Kiryu had some strange, terrifying and distressing dreams in his life, but
that dream had felt eerily real.

It was while he was pondering the nature of that weird dream that there was a knock at his
door.

Kiryu's gaze drifted towards the source of the sound, only to be met by the sight of Haruka
poking her head through the gap. Her waking him up was nothing new, but for some reason,
there was a strange look in her eyes today.

"Uncle Kaz," Haruka called to him softly. "There's someone here to see you. She says she has
an appointment?"

Kiryu sighed, pushing himself up from his bed. Now that he thought about it, he did have an
appointment scheduled for today. It was a rather important one too, and he'd almost slept
through it.

He spared a small, tired smile for Haruka.



"Thank you, Haruka," Kiryu murmured, his voice carrying a hint of tiredness. "I'll be dressed
in a minute. Please show her in and prepare some tea."

With a nod, Haruka disappeared from the doorway, leaving Kiryu alone with his thoughts
once more. Not for too long though. He didn't want to keep his guest waiting after all.

After getting out of bed, slipping into his usual hawaiian shirt and making himself look
somewhat presentable, he headed to the dining room.

Once he arrived, he saw Haruka tending to a mature-looking woman. White coat, short brown
hair, black skirt and a serious expression that probably would have had some yakuza crossing
the street to avoid her.

Everything about this woman screamed "business," or "don't mess with me," and Kiryu
wasn't too sure if he was in any condition to be dealing with someone like her.

As Haruka filled the woman's cup with some simple green tea, the lady's gaze drifted to meet
his own.

"Ah, Kazuma Kiryu, I presume?" The woman asked. She quickly procured a business card
and placed it on the table. "Mirei Park of Dyna Chair Talent Agency. Why don't you take a
seat? We've much to discuss..."

Yeah, Kiryu wasn't looking forward to this meeting at all.

The room was silent as a grave.

There were no accurate words to describe how everyone was feeling after watching that
display. The stupor in the air was palpable.

And could anyone be blamed for that? They had just watched a young man erupt into blue
flames and then singlehandedly defeat dozens of knights in an instant.

And now that man was gripping the wrist of a rapidly paling king.

"Y-You...what the h-hell...?" Kamoshida stuttered. How was this even possible!? This kid
had been on death's door not a few minutes ago, and now he pulls the same power as the
Sakamoto peasant and took out an entire squad of knights in the literal blink of an eye?

He was expecting a power upgrade; that's what happened with Sakamoto after all, but
this...this was almost completely different! It was like comparing the Earth to the moon.

Gone were the wounds he inflicted, and gone was that ravaged uniform. In their place was a
black and white domino mask and an outfit that had a similar feel to Sakamoto's.

His outfit consisted of mostly black gentlemen's garb with a high collar and low coattails,
split into three, tossed over a grey waistcoat. With black pants and dark, pointy, steel-lined
shoes, he could almost be described as a shadow. But the comparison stopped with his
gloves.



They were a deep crimson red and didn't appear as sleek as the rest of his attire. They fit him
perfectly, but they were just a bit thicker than normal gloves, like motorcycle gloves. Metal
inlets lined his knuckles and fingers, likely to give his punches more power.

But the most eye-catching details of his new attire weren't the gloves or the mask.

It was the pure white embroidery that had been sewn into the outfit's back. It covered the
entire expanse with a single, intimidating design.

 

That of a dragon.

 

A nobori-ryu style dragon to be precise. A divine, serpentine figure soaring skywards to
pierce the heavens. A being with inexplicable power...

 

Now in the hands of the mortal boy who dared wield it.

 

It was contradictory to have something so bold embroidered onto something that was
supposed to be suave. And yet Akira felt so right wearing it.

The sheer power rolling off of the young man would render even the most hardened of people
speechless, to say nothing of the two teens, one king and a cat.

With his confidence completely shattered after Akira's display, Kamoshida frantically tried to
wrench his arm away from Akira. "L-Let go! Dammit, let go!" He snarled.

He may as well have been ensnared by a statue. Akira's grip didn't budge at all.

The teen said nothing in reply. He didn't have words to give for the scumbag in his grip.
Instead, he turned his head to Ann, who was on the floor, staring up at him. She looked
confused, stunned even. But there was no mistaking her relief now that she was out of
Kamoshida's clutches.

"Takamaki, go back to Morgana and Sakamoto." He ordered. "You don't want to be close by
when I turn this bastard inside out..." He told her in a low voice.

Ann found herself doubtless that Akira could do so, and so she managed to scramble to her
feet. She then proceeded to back away from the evil king and the masked teen.

"T-Turn me inside out...?!" Kamoshida squeaked. He completely believed that if he didn't
get free, the would indeed be facing that.

Desperately, he wound his free arm back and punched Akira in the back of the head.



 

To his horror, the kid didn't even move.

 

"You just keep digging yourself deeper and deeper, don't you?" Akira turned back to him,
fury in his eyes. "That's fine, I wasn't planning on pulling my punches anyway..."

"Y-You...you can't do this...! I'm the ki--OMMMMPFF!!"

The next thing he knew, Kamoshida felt a crushing pain in his gut, and black blood flew from
his mouth. Black blood was a detail that Akira would initially question but right now he was
too pissed off to wonder how that was possible. He just reasoned that black blood was fitting
for a person with a black heart.

The strike winded the king beyond description, folding into a 90 degree angle. The pain had
him grasping his gut as if he'd just been stabbed. And yet, without pause, Akira lifted his leg
high into the air and brought it down hard on the king's head, slamming him down on the
floor.

The piddly little crown atop the scumbag's head went flying across the room, clanking as it
bounced away.

 

"Essence of Continuous Slams..."

 

Kamoshida didn't even have the chance to reach for it before his unkempt hair was grabbed.
His head was lifted up and then harshly brought down again, shattering the floor tiles.

The king gasped in pain, and yet he couldn't even collect himself before his head was
slammed again.

 

And again...

 

And again...

 

And again...

 



Even when there were audible cracks and the man's pathetic flailing started to dwindle, Akira
didn't stop. Even when a thin puddle of black appeared under the king's head, Akira didn't
stop. And only when the man started to whimper in agony did Akira's slamming cease...

Only to be replaced by a harsh, kick to his face, with his steel-toed shoes. The impact caused
Kamoshida to spin in place on the floor while also knocking out a few of his teeth.

Overwhelmed with pain and fear at this point, Kamoshida could barely move. This kid had
gone from being a stain on the floor to a force of nature. If only he could enter his Asmodeus
form...!

Delusions of doing so left him as Akira mounted the broken king and painfully dug his knees
into his back. And then he grabbed his right arm.

"You know, you took away Sakamoto's ability to run for shits and giggles, right?" Akira
asked as he pulled the king's arm up behind his back before he started bending it the wrong
way.

"...To be honest, I can't think of a greater irony than having the same kind of thing happen to
you!"

"Ah! Wauuit! Waauuuiii--!!"

 

SNAP

 

"AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAGHHH!!"

 

Akira's silent yet brutal strength had no difficulties in snapping Kamoshida's arm in two. Not
even a small break. As sickening as it was, Akira pulled and pulled until it was bent all the
way down to the elbow, in a state that looked like a mistreated action figure.

The man's face was now covered in a mix of black blood, tears and snot. The face of a
coward that, until now, had gone unopposed, finally finding out that he wasn't as big a fish as
he thought.

He was beaten. There was no point in sticking around anymore. Honestly, Akira was half
tempted to just run his fist through the king's prick face and hoped he vanished like those
knights did, but something told him going that far would be different to the knights. So he
refrained.

"You'd better pray that we don't ever meet again. Unless you want me to make what I did
today look like a playground scuffle." Akira hissed. He wasn't sure if the man actually heard
him but he got his point across either way.



He got up from the king, letting the man snivel and whimper, cradling his broken arm. He
then jumped over the balcony, forgoing the stairs in exchange for a landing that was seeping
with power.

He raised his head and glanced at his allies, who admittedly looked a little uncomfortable. He
didn't blame them. He hadn't exactly been subtle. Or merciful.

Yet the awed looks did make him feel pretty good about himself. He let out a sigh.

 

Everyone was safe.

 

"Sorry you had to see that," Akira told the trio.

"Uh...it's...alright, dude?" The skull-masked blonde replied.

Akira nodded and ran his hand through his frizzy hair. Today had been a long day, and it was
only going to get longer. They were incredibly, incredibly late for school, and they wouldn't
have any time to process any of this. But that was a matter that could be dealt with later.
Right now, it was time to finally leave this godforsaken castle.

As he walked towards the front door, he gestured for the others to follow, which they did with
various expressions of relief. They had been backed into a corner, wounded, and
outnumbered, but today, they walked away victors.

"S-So...argh..." Ryuji grunted as he was pulled along by Ann. "You got a Persona thingy too,
huh?" He asked.

"Seems like it," Akira answered. "Hurt like hell though."

"Dude, I know! Felt like my brain was being boiled!" Ryuji replied.

Morgana looked up at the group. He couldn't believe that he'd found not one, but two Persona
users, in the same day no less!

 

He could work with this...

 

"S-So um..." Ann spoke up. "Who'd you roll?" She asked.

"Pardon?" Akira replied.

"You know... Morgana's got Zorro. And Ryuji's got the Captain guy. So, what about you?"
She pressed.



"Dude, this ain't some kind of gacha game..." Ryuji commented with a shake of his head.

Akira took a moment to close his eyes, and for an instant, something rose up within him, as if
from the depths of his soul. A chortle of malefic laughter. It brought a small smile to his face,
despite its sinister nature. He felt comforted by the presence.

"So, who is it?" Ann asked once more.

Akira was silent for a second, and he looked up at the front door. He took a small breath,
reared his foot back and rammed it into them.

With a deafening crash, the previously locked doors were flung open. They both probably
weighed a ton, and yet Akira's strike busted it open as if it were a regular door. Morgana,
Ryuji and Ann were completely stunned at the display. Morgana himself believed that they
would have needed to double back and try another exit, but Akira evidently wanted out of
here now.

As the massive doors slammed open and revealed the outside world, Akira turned to look at
the group before him with a satisfied smile.

 

"Arsène Lupin, the Pillager of Twilight."

The alleyway was a welcome sight, and once the group of teens scrambled through it, their
ears were blessed with the sounds of traffic, people chatting and whatever the electronic
advertisements were peddling.

As they sprung out into the long-since dried street, it became quickly apparent that some
things had changed.

For one, Akira and Ryuji's clothes had changed back to their normal school uniforms.
Additionally, the damage that had been done to both their clothes and their bodies had been
undone, as if it had never happened. And yet, both of them felt completely exhausted.

The sky had returned to a normal blue, and people were wandering about without a care in
the world, completely oblivious to the unbelievable events that had happened to the youths.

"We're..back...?" Ryuji whispered. He staggered on his feet and leaned himself up against a
wall.

"Oh thank God!" Ann gasped, immediately falling to her knees. They had been through so
much in the past couple of hours, so once they were safe, all that tension was released in one
go.

Akira then felt a small buzz in his pocket and dug inside to pull out his phone. Curiously, the
screen was once again taken up by that red and black eyeball.

 



"You have returned to the real world. Welcome back."

 

Returned to the real world? The hell did that mean?

"This is...the real world...?" A familiar, high-pitched voice spoke from behind them.

That was... Morgana's voice, wasn't it? But in the populated streets, something like a bipedal
feline that looked like it was ripped from an anime would doubtlessly draw heads.

In a fluster, the trio turned to hide the presumed monster cat...

Only to find an ordinary black and white cat staring out from the alleyway with curious,
bright blue eyes. They were confused.

"Eh...? For a second there, I could've sworn I heard Mangora..." Ryuji muttered.

The cat's back suddenly arched and its expression turned angry. "My name is MORGANA!
Get it right, you moron!" The cat cried.

 

Cried...

 

With a voice that was no less human than their own.

 

For a few seconds, the teens stared at the cat hard enough to make his fur bristle. "Wh-
What...?" He asked defensively.

"I...don't know if it's 'cuz I'm tired as shit, or something...but is anyone else...?" Ryuji looked
to the other two.

"Yep," Akira answered.

"Th-The cat's talking..." Ann whispered, eyes wide in disbelief. It was a look shared by the
guys too.

Because holy fuck, a cat was talking to them! They thought the weird stuff would stop as
soon as they left the castle!

"I'm not a cat!" Morgana insisted. "I didn't have time to explain this before, but I somehow
lost my original form. I may be in the body of a cat, but I'm not one!" Morgana explained.

"W-Well, whatever it is, it's creepy, not to mention not normal for a cat to speak. Don't go
talkin' willy-nilly when there are other people around!" Ryuji frantically pointed to the exit of
the alleyway where people were walking past.



A man and woman took that moment to walk past the alleyway.

"Is that a cat I hear...?" The woman asked.

"Urgh, the amount of stray cats nowadays. Can't walk past any alleyway without hearing their
stupid meowing."

The statement, mean-spirited as it was, brought with it a certain important detail. They heard
meowing? But Morgana was speaking like a person.

 

Unless...

 

"Meowing? Wait, does that mean that we can understand you, but others can't?" Ryuji asked.
Akira and Ann had reached a similar conclusion

"Seems so." Akira chimed in. "We're some kind of cat-whispering super trio now." He sighed
and rested his head against the wall. "Anyway, Morgana?"

"Yeah?"

"I'm sure that you probably have better things to do than tell us exactly what we just
experienced, but I feel like we need some answers. Like, now."

"Yeah!" Ryuji piped up. "Like what the hell was going on with that castle, and Kamoshida,
and us!? Our Personas? Gah, I have so many questions!" He groaned.

"Well..."

"And could it be the short version, please? We have to get to school soon." Akira interrupted.

"Shit, man...school? Ah, I'm so tired. I just wanna go home and crash. It'll be miserable!"
Ryuji moaned at the reminder

"If there's a Poppo on the way, we can grab a Staminan X or something," Akira suggested.

"Huh? Those energy drinks? They're so expensive!" Ryuji objected.

"They work, though. You buy it, and we can split it between us." Akira replied.

"Eh, why do I gotta buy it?" The blond asked with an offended look on his face.

"Because I'm broke, because I saved your asses in that castle, and because I won't stop calling
you 'Princess Ryuji' if you don't," Akira answered in a deadpan voice.

"Wh-Huh...!? You...! Argh, fine! But you better pay me back!" Ryuji spluttered before he
bitterly pulled his wallet out of his pocket and began to gauge his allowance.



"Do you guys want an explanation or not!?" Morgana shouted. They had the gall to request
an explanation and then not listen to him?

"Ah, sorry!" Akira yelped. It was a weird sight to see a cat visibly angry as if it had a human
face.

The trio leaned forward and listened as the cat began to speak.

Even though they had asked for the short version, Morgana's explanation had taken over five
minutes. It was sort of a mishmash of terminologies, but Akira could somewhat process it.

As he understood, he, Ryuji and Ann somehow ended up in a place called the Metaverse, an
alternate reality running parallel to their own. It was a world based on cognition. When
somebody in the real world developed "distorted desires," there was a chance that they could
develop a 'Palace', a stronghold of their subconscious will and things that they'd rather keep
secret.

The Palace would usually superimpose itself over a location the individual believed they had
authority over, and would be moulded by how that individual saw that location.

Beings known as Shadows were drawn to these Palaces, and took the form of what was most
appropriate for that Palace, such as the knights in the castle. But they could also enter a
stronger, more versatile form. These Shadows were dominated and controlled by the main
Shadow, a reflection of how the individual saw themselves. Kamoshida was a teacher in the
real world, but his reputation and freedom to do what he wished in reality caused him to
manifest his own Shadow in the form of a king.

The worlds were separate, but they were still connected. Something happening in the real
world could affect a related thing in the Metaverse if the individual was made aware of the
change. The world was again, based off of cognition.

And if someone just so happen to slip into a Palace, a Persona was how they would defend
themselves. They were a reflection of the person's inner rebel. A variable that the Palace
Ruler was unable to control. Normally, a Persona was the only way that one was able to fight
Shadows, and the clothes that they wore were like suits of armour to defend against the
Palace's dangers.

That was about as far as Morgana got before Ryuji gave a wail and cradled his head.

"Aaaargh! There's no way this is the short version! Everything you just told us is too frickin'
confusing! I'm not gonna be able to remember all of this!" He cried.

"Sakamoto! You're making a scene!" Ann hissed from her spot on the ground. People were
going to peer into the alleyway and see the display. It would be mortifying! Sure, this was
complicated, and she might need a second listen to comprehend everything, but Ryuji was
overreacting.

"I'm trying to make this as brief and as simple as possible! It's not my fault that your monkey
brain can't wrap your head around it!" Morgana huffed.



"What did you say, you little--?"

"Sakamoto, stop it. We didn't leave that castle so we could throw hands at each other. You
need to calm down." Akira stepped in, placing a hand on Ryuji's shoulder. He then looked at
Morgana with a displeased expression.

"And you, Morgana, not everyone catches up the same way. This is all pretty bizarre, but you
don't have to be mean when people don't get it right away." He told the cat.

"Well, you get it, don't you, Frizzy Hair?" Morgana asked, narrowing his eyes.

"Sorta?" Akira replied. "It's a little bit loaded, but I think I got the gist of it. Basically,
Kamoshida's Shadow, the castle and everything is a sadistic power fantasy of the real
Kamoshida, come to life in another dimension, right?"

"Uh, pretty much," Morgana replied.

"That makes a little more sense, I guess..." Ryuji admitted. "If anyone is deluded enough to
see the school like that, it'd be effin' Kamoshida."

"Good, now you get it! I mean, there are a lot of details being left out, but that's the general
basics of what you just experienced." Morgana said.

"So, all those knights I beat up, they should have been stronger?" Akira asked.

Morgana muttered something under his breath before he nodded. "They should have been
able to use the full scope of their abilities. They would have appeared as different creatures
with different abilities and resistances. Chances are that if they transformed, you could have
come across one that could totally negate your physical strikes. As knights though, they
weren't strong or weak against anything. By the time that you were truly recognised as a
threat, it was too late for them to transform." Morgana explained.

"I see," Akira replied. "And when I beat Shadow Kamoshida black and blue, that didn't have
any effect on the real Kamoshida? The real him wouldn't have any memory of what happened
in the palace?" He asked.

"Maybe he got a muscle spasm or something, but nothing serious. And no, people aren't privy
to what happens in the Metaverse, even if they have a Palace." Morgana answered.

Akira honestly didn't know if he was relieved or upset at that info.

"But what I wanna know is--"

"U-Um...guys? Fourth period's almost over. If we hang around any longer, we'll miss lunch."
Ann interrupted the cat. She held up her phone and showed a reminder in her Calender app
that said "Lunch" followed by several food and heart emojis.

"You have a reminder for lunch?" Ryuji asked.



"I...used to eat my food in between classes, and would have nothing left for lunch, so I started
leaving myself a reminder so I didn't eat everything I had," Ann admitted.

Hm, so Ann had an appetite, huh? Akira didn't expect that from her. She seemed to put in a
lot of effort in maintaining herself.

"Well then, we need to get moving." Akira reasoned. Being this late on his first day, he'd
make a terrible first impression...

Actually, Shadow Kamoshida mentioned something to the effect of spreading his criminal
record around, didn't he?

 

Shit.

 

"Oh right...uh, you gonna be okay, dude?" Ryuji asked. It was clear that he'd come to the
same conclusion. "I don't know if you heard after you got--"

"Yeah, I heard..." Akira sighed. He really couldn't catch a break. "How's...the rumour mill at
Shujin?"

Ryuji lowered his head and ruffled his fingers through his bleached hair. "Brutal." He
answered. "Rumours spread like wildfire there. When I came back after Kamoshida broke my
leg, everyone was sayin' that I was some kind of punk. And Takamaki... Right now, everyone
thinks that she's hooking up with Kamoshida."

"Hey! Don't say that out loud!" Ann hissed. She recoiled from those words like they
physically burned her.

"What? We know the truth, don't we? It was pretty clear in Kamoshida's Palace thingy." He
told her.

"Still..." Ann muttered before she buried her head into her knees.

Akira cursed under his breath. It seemed that Kamoshida had turned all three of their school
lives into an ordeal in one way or another, be it spreading rumours or the like. How had he
been allowed to go this far? They were students for goodness sake!

"Same goes for you too, man. Even if people say that you got a criminal record, we know that
you're actually pretty nice." Ryuji told Akira, patting him on the shoulder.

"I guess...thanks, Sakamoto." Akira sighed. There was some small solace in that fact, he
supposed. Still, the fact that the entirety of the student body would know of his unjust record
would weigh heavily on him.

"Hey, no problem. And you know what? Call me Ryuji. We've fought together in that Palace
thing, so I think we're past using those formalities. I'll call you Akira, yeah?" The blond



chattered enthusiastically.

That declaration took Akira by surprise. Ryuji's definition of fighting together aside, nobody
had ever had the chance to be close enough to Akira to use his first name, minus Morning
Glory, of course.

So...they were friends now? Huh? Well, Akira couldn't deny a warm, fuzzy feeling in his
chest when Ryuji so proudly said that. He thought that it maybe happened a little fast, but
near-death experiences did have a way of bringing people closer.

"You...You can call me Ann as well!" Ann suddenly spoke. "I-I hope we can get along,
Akira!" She made an attempt at a smile.

Ann too? That... he didn't expect that either, but...it was nice. Quite nice. "I-I got it. Ryuji and
Ann it is." Akira said with a nod.

"Well with that outta the way, let's get going then!" Ryuji proclaimed as he stumbled towards
the exit. "I really hope that this Staminan stuff works as well as you're making it out to be,
because dammit, I can barely move..." He sighed. He then looked to Ann, still sat on the
ground. "Hey, Ann--"

And that was when they realised that Ann was shaking. Her arms gripped her legs tightly, and
she was pressed against the alley wall like she was trying to burrow into it.

It was immediately obvious that something was wrong, and Akira was pretty sure he knew
what it was. After the experience that Ann had inside the Palace, he'd be surprised if she
came out the other side without some form of trauma.

"Hey, Ryuji? Why don't you find a Poppo and wait for us there? We'll catch up in a bit."
Akira asked the blond.

For his moments of obliviousness, Ryuji definitely knew that something was going on.
Something that he sure as hell wouldn't be able to assist with. He'd make himself more useful
by doing what Akira asked of him.

 

But still...

 

"You sure, dude?" He asked. And Akira nodded back.

After a moment of contemplation, Ryuji silently agreed. "Okay. I'll wait on you guys then..."
He replied, shuffling his way out of the alleyway.

"And watch out for truancy officers!" Akira called out to Ryuji. This day sucked enough
without the law getting on their asses.



Ryuji gave him a thumbs-up before finally disappearing around the corner. As his footsteps
slowly blended into the traffic, Akira turned his attention towards Ann, concern etched on his
face.

He walked up to the girl and started to sit down next to her. "Okay, Ann. Do you want to--"

Before he could sit down though, he noticed Ann flinch. For a moment, he was confused
until he remembered what Ann was likely going through.

She probably didn't want to be within reaching distance of any male.

"Sorry!" He apologized, relocating himself so he was a little ways down the alley. "I'll keep
my distance..." He assured the twintailed girl.

"Don't be," Ann assured Akira, her voice trembling slightly. "It's not your fault."

"Lady Ann..." Morgana whispered.

For about a minute, the guy, the girl and the cat sat there in silence. It was obvious what they
should talk about. But Ann... It was better if she made the first move.

"When...when we were in that castle," she started to say, "we all saw what Kamoshida saw
me as...we all heard what he wanted...t-to do to me..."

The horrific, mortifying fake of hers clinging clumsily onto Kamoshida. The way that they
made out with her just inches away, helpless to do anything...those petrifying memories
repeated over and over in her mind.

"I...I don't want to go there. I don't want to go to a place where that bastard has all the power
in the world. He comes onto me all the time whenever he sees me in the hall." She put on a
fake, deep voice. "Do you need a ride home, Takamaki-san?", "Do you want to eat lunch
together, Takamaki-san?" And now, knowing exactly how he sees me; what he wants with
me..."

 

The weight of Ann's unspoken words hung heavy in the air.

 

Silently, Akira listened, his eyes filled with empathy, as Ann described her situation in full.
The persistent advances, the suggestive comments, the unwelcome invitations—it was a
constant barrage that filled her with anxiety and fear. Now, with Kamoshida's true intentions
out in the open, those emotions were multiplied tenfold. Ann was trapped and she was
desperate.

"You're scared," Akira stated softly. "With every reason to be."

Tears welled up in Ann's eyes as she nodded. "I-I could just go home, right? Call in sick?"
She asked with a quiet voice. "I feel like I'm going to throw up anyway…"



Akira sighed, his gaze unwavering. "I wish that could fix your problem. But Kamoshida
won't go away just because you take a day or two off school."

The tremor in Ann's voice reflected her uncertainty. "Yeah, I know. But I... I don't know what
to do," she confessed, her voice tinged with helplessness. "All I want is for him to leave me
alone. I want him to forget about me... but…"

"He won't," Akira interrupted, his voice firm yet sympathetic. Dammit, if he wasn't on
probation, he'd march right up to the bastard and wring his neck with his own volleyball net.
