It went by the name of Untouchable Airsoft, a store that peddled replica weapons. Everything
from blades, maces and lots of guns. Most of them were made out of foam, some out of
aluminium. The damage they could do in an actual brawl was laughable at best.

But in the Metaverse, it was different. Cognition of the weapons gave them life, gave them
power, made them real. They didn't need to be real, just look real. Cognition would do the
rest.

That didn't change the fact that Untouchable filled Akira with a sense of unease, and it wasn't
because of the dim lighting, the faux firearms lining the walls or the smell of smoke and oil.

No, it had to do with the grizzled store owner chewing on a lollipop whose stare hadn't left
Akira since he walked in. He was looking at him like he was a punk who had robbed his
grandma. Not that Akira couldn't relate. His instincts were firing on all cylinders in regard to
the man. That he was no ordinary shopkeeper. Just like Akira was no ordinary customer.

There was an unspoken tension between the pair that Ryuji seemed to be ignorant of as he
went through several shotguns while going "pow-pow!"

Akira did his best to ignore the gaze digging into his back as he tried to focus on the rack of
submachine guns. Ann's aim with his pistol was abysmal, so something that was low damage
but high volume that she could spray-and-pray seemed ideal for her. It would have been
easier if she was here, but she had to work on her homework with Shiho. Not to mention that
she probably wouldn't feel like being around any more shady men...

But pretending like there wasn't anything going on didn't make the animosity disappear, and
it was only a matter of time before the tension reached its peak.

"Hey, Blondie," the store owner growled at Ryuji. "Mind stepping outside for a few minutes?
I need to have a...word with your friend."



"Huh? Something wrong?" Ryuji asked, handling one of the prop shotguns in a way that had
the owner suppressing a cringe.

Ryuji, you use a shotgun in battle; how can you not tell which end the bullets come out of!?

"Ryuji..." The blond glanced at Akira. "Do what he says. I'll explain later." He stared firmly.
He slipped off his Morgana-laden backpack and handed it to the confused boy. The last thing
he wanted was to get anyone innocent get caught in the crossfire.

"Erm...o-okay, I guess..." Ryuji replied. He didn't know what was going on but the
seriousness in Akira's voice indicated that he should listen. He placed the shotgun on the rack
(upside-down) and slipped out of the store.

Now it was just him and the owner...

"Fuck sent you?"

Akira blinked. "Excuse me?"

 

[Substory 12: A Gecko in Hand]

 

"Don't play dumb." The man spat. "You've had an air around you ever since you walked in. I
could never mistake it." He spoke gruffly. "What family wants beef with me now?
Shimamoto? Okanda? Or are you one of Tsuda's boys?"

"Sir, I think you have the wrong idea about me. I'm just here to buy some model guns." Akira
replied calmly, even as his fists tightened.

"Bullshit." The owner replied. "There's only one type of person that has the same aura as you.
And those kinds of people aren't allowed in my shop."

"And I'm telling you that you're mistaken. Whatever kind of person you think I am, I'm not.
You're gonna refuse me service just because you feel like it?" Akira asked, taking a step
forward. He noticed the man tense up. Not in a frightened way, but in preparation to throw
down. It was obvious by now that this man was no stranger to violence.

"I've a right to refuse service to anyone that I please. And I can tell that you're trouble. Even
if I sold something to you, you'd probably start waving it around in people's faces. You'd get
the cops' attention; they'd trace your purchase back to here. I don't need any heat..."

"That's not true!" Akira protested. "I'm just a gun enthusiast!"

And Shadows weren't people!

"True or not, I'm not selling my stock to someone like you. Too risky, obviously a bad deal."
He muttered as he got up off of his chair and leaned on the display cabinet menacingly,



matching Akira's glare. He reached into one of his coat pockets and fiddled with something.
Something that became evident as Akira heard a near-silent whirr from the ceiling as the only
security camera in the shop powered down.

"Now, I'm gonna have to ask you to leave. I don't want to have to get physical, but if I have to
toss you out on your ass, I will." He warned.

"Tch!" Akira clicked his teeth. This was bullshit! The bastard didn't even know him, and he
was straight up refusing to sell him anything, going as far as to threaten actual harm all
because he didn't meet some imaginary non-punk quota! The man looked way shadier than he
did!

It was a kind of injustice that Akira had unfortunately grown quite familiar with during the
preceding weeks. At the very least the guy seemed to want to keep the police out of it. A
single report like that would immediately void his probation, regardless of who the aggressor
was.

The smart move would be to do what the man said: to leave, to try to find another place that
sold model guns. But Akira doubted that there would be anywhere that provided a selection
as realistic as Untouchable's, and when it came to the Metaverse, the more realistic the
weapon, the better. With how high the stakes were, everyone having the best gear possible
was paramount.

So Akira planted his feet and took a steadying breath. This guy seemed like the kind who
couldn't be convinced with words. Fine. Then that meant his mouth's job was done. Time for
his fists to take over.

He slowly slipped into the Brawler Style. "I'm sorry. But I'm not leaving until you sell me
those guns. If you wanna toss me out, then...then..." Akira quirked a brow. "Uh...are you
okay?"

He didn't know what the heck happened, but the moment he got ready to fight, the owner's
countenance reformed from gruff and threatening to astonishment and disbelief. The lollipop
that he was chewing on fell from his gaping mouth and clattered on the floor.

Then he whispered something that Akira could barely make out, but shook his whole world
all the same.

 

"Dragon of Dojima..."

 

It was impossible for Akira to forget that title. The one that The Florist had dropped on him
back when he made that fateful phone call back in Okinawa. 'Dragon of Dojima'. There was
only one man that could hold the weight of such a name.

"You...You know Kiryu Kazuma?" Akira asked.



The man blinked once and his expression hardened once more. "Only met him once a couple
decades ago but he's not a guy you forget easily..." He replied.

He of course neglected to mention that their meeting had been short-lived and hardly pleasant
as he was just a common shakedowner on the streets of Kamurocho. He'd been harassing a
random salaryman while the white-suited man was passing by, and he, to put it succinctly, got
his ass handed to him.

 

The speed.

 

The power.

 

By the time he had realised that he had picked a fight with the wrong man, Kiryu had already
picked up a damn bicycle and fucking powerbombed it over his chest. His ribs had taken
three months to properly heal...

That man was a force of nature in snakeskin shoes, and the way he had fought had been quite
literally beaten into his memory. Which only begged the question:

 

"Who the hell are you, kid?"

"I'm...just someone whose only chance of survival depended on what I could learn from
someone like Kiryu-san." Akira answered solemnly. He averted his gaze to look at a rack of
pistols on the wall. "I watched him a lot when he first came to town. Used what I learned to
protect myself, to stand up to the kind of people that he stood up to. He doesn't know me,
but...I'm alive because of him."

Better to let this guy think that he was just some random dude that didn't even exist to Kiryu.
The fewer connections made, the better.

The man stayed silent for a few seconds before he reached up and adjusted his cap. "You look
up to him, huh?" The owner pressed.

Akira nodded.

"...you really should pick your role models better, kid. A former yakuza isn't the kind of
person you should be learning from if you wanna live decently."

Akira swallowed down the urge to jump to Kiryu's defence. That man, knowingly or not, had
taught him how to fight. He had put a roof over his head, gave him a family; a purpose
beyond surviving on the street from one day to the next.



Kiryu might have been a former yakuza, but he was undoubtedly a great man.

"Haah...look..." The owner sighed as he sat back down in his chair. "Kiryu Kazuma is a
legend amongst legends in the underground. He's done things that the normal rank wouldn't
even dream of. You gotta be careful who you get into scuffles with, cuz the kind of attention
that guy gets is never the good kind." He said with a resigned tone. "I mean, you got that aura
of his secondhand and it was still enough for me to try and bite your head off."

The man dug into his other pocket and produced a fresh lollipop, which he yanked the
wrapper off before popping it into his mouth.

"I think that we might have gotten off on the wrong foot. I mean, you're still shady as hell,
but as long as you don't bring any trouble down on me, we shouldn't have a problem."

Akira allowed himself a surprised reaction. "So...does that mean...?

The man grinned. "How about we start over? I'm Munehisa Iwai. Welcome to my humble
shop. How can I help you today?"

To say that dealing with Iwai had been shaky at the start was an understatement, but once
everything was cleared up, Akira found himself in decently good company. Iwai was bit of a
wiseass and rough around the edges, but it was preferable to the hostility from earlier. It
probably helped that Akira wasn't handling the fake firearms like a drunken monkey (cough
cough Ryuji cough cough).

Iwai had even taken the time to point out the different kinds of model guns and weapons that
he had for sale. Some were dirt cheap and others were so expensive that Akira's glasses
almost cracked when he looked at the price tag.

"Quality costs. Don't like it? Sell a kidney." Iwai said.

Akira had flipped him the bird.

"Beautiful finger. Mean that honestly." Iwai had replied.

They got along like peas and carrots.

Akira left the store with a few model guns for the team. It ate through nearly all the cash
they'd amassed so far, but they were already better than what they currently had.

He had to skimp on melee weapons though. Shame, he really liked those knuckledusters...

But nonetheless, he knew he'd be coming back here.

The weird voice in his head told him so!

Two days.



It had taken two days for the security level in the Palace to normalise after the shitshow that
became of the previous infiltration. Considering that they were on a time crunch, it made
them a little antsy.

Thankfully, things in Kamoshida's palace had mostly returned to normal. There were no
guards or dragons in sight, though there were several parts of the exterior wall in need of
repair. Drago-Kira really did a number on this place.

It was up in the air whether that was going to make the infiltration easier or harder.

But now, here the gang stood, as kitted out as they could be and one member stronger.

Speaking of which...

"Ugh... Why'd mine have to be a catsuit...? I feel so exposed." Ann moaned. She hadn't
noticed it before under the din of conflict, but she was now very aware that her outfit looked
like something she'd see on a billboard in a red-light district.

Her head whipped around to see a certain cat and blond blatantly ogling her. "Will you two
perverts stop looking!?" She cried.

"Bwah! I wasn't lookin'!" Ryuji yelped.

He had totally been looking...

"You can't choose how your outfit manifests when you awaken to a Personas. They take the
form of whatever you believe best represents an image of rebellion." Morgana explained.
Then with a predictable swooning motion, Morgana grinned at the abashed girl. "But I for
one think you look positively radiant, Lady Ann!"

Never before had she heard a compliment that made her wanna fling the recipient into the sky
by their pointy ears...

Ann rolled her eyes and turned to the lone gentleman in the group. Akira was kneeling at the
entryway, keeping an eye on the guard detail. A few Shadows marched by the castle doors,
but they weren't anything to worry about. Their infiltration point was still secure.

"Oh yeah! You need a codename, as well, don'tcha?" Ryuji exclaimed. Ann turned to him in
mild bewilderment. "Codename?" She asked.

"We can't go around shouting our actual names in the Palace. It could have an adverse effect
on Kamoshida in reality. Plus, it's way cooler!" Morgana declared.

"Huh..." Ann placed a gloved hand on her hip. "What are your codenames then?"

Ryuji reached up and tapped at his mask. "I'm Skull! Sounds badass, right? Matches my mask
and everything!"

"Che! I still think 'Thug' worked better." Morgana tutted as he crossed his tiny arms.



Normally, that would've raised Ryuji's hackles, but the blond just chucked. "And I think
'Kitty Kat' worked better for you. Why'd we settle on 'Mona' again?"

Morgana's fur bristled and he gnashed his teeth at Ryuji. "I am NOT a cat! Or a 'Kitty Kat', or
whatever else you blockheads came up with!"

Ann sighed as the pair began to squabble back and forth. If the commotion brought the
guards over to them, Ann swore that she'd kill both of them before the Shadows could...

She looked back to Akira, who had moved on to doing stretches to limber up for the coming
Palace crawl. "What about you? What's your codename?" She asked. Though truth be told, it
was probably pointless. There was only one codename that he could possibly--

"Joker."

"Eh?" Ann squeaked. That was... unexpected. Beyond unexpected. "B-But the dragon on
your back...a-and you're so strong!"

How could he be anything other than 'Dragon'?

"That's what I said!" Ryuji announced while pulling on Morgana's cheeks. "But he said he
didn't want it. Somethin' about "not being worthy" or whatev--yooow!"

Ryuji dropped Morgana as the cat kicked him in the chin. The cat massaged his abused
cheeks a little before interjecting. "I came up with Joker. He's like a super trump card." Then
the cat seemed to trail away. "But Dragon would've been cool too..."

Well ultimately, it was Akira's choice on what name he was running around with. If he didn't
wanna be called Dragon, there was nothing they could do. It wasn't like Joker didn't also have
its charm.

"W-Well...if I need to have a codename..." Ann looked down at the costume that represented
her rebellious spirit. It was what she had to work with. "Gotta be something to do with a cat."
She muttered.

"Cougar~"

Ann was going to punt that cat into the damn stratosphere.

"Well, we are s'posed to be stealing a treasure, so...what about Cat Burglar?"

A little better, but not exactly as ferocious as she was hoping.

"I came up with a few names if you're interested," Akira mentioned as he dug into his pocket
and retrieved a small slip of paper he'd probably torn from his probation diary.

"Huh, when did you do that?" Morgana questioned.

"Last night, when you went to sleep!"



What!? I thought you went to sleep too! I told you that it was time for bed!"

"Sorry. Rebellious spirit and all that~" Akira teased. He walked over to Ann and handed the
list to her. The girl was quick to peruse her options.

 

Tigress, Lioness, Cheetah...

Definitely cool sounding.

 

Scarlet, Ruby, Cinder...

Something to do with her colour scheme or her fire. Not a bad idea but it didn't hit as hard as
Joker and Skull.

 

Selena, Felicia, Belladonna...

Those were just kind of...names. where had Akira gotten them from?

 

Panther...

 

...

 

Yeah. That sounded right.

"I'll be Panther." She declared while handing the list back to Akira. It was ferocious, went
with the feline theme, and rolled off the tongue easy.

"Awlright! From here on out, we're gonna be Joker, Skull, Mona and Panther. Four heroes
against the world!" Ryuji pumped his fist. The excitement was pouring off of him. After
everything that happened, they were finally going to make their first serious shot to conquer
the Palace.

"Don't get too worked up, we barely got started last time." Morgana tutted. "I don't need to
remind any of you of how dangerous this is. We need to be vigilant the entire time we're in
there. We watch each other's backs, keep our heads cool and pick our battles where we can."

Morgana adjusted his small utility belt and looked up to the top of the castle. "My guess is
that the treasure is held somewhere at the top of the castle. We're definitely not gonna make it
that far in just one day, but as long as we--"



"No."

Morgana fell silent as Akira unexpectedly spoke up. Everyone looked at him curiously.

"Sorry Mona, but we can't take our time here. Every moment we dawdle is more time for
Kamoshida to get back on his feet." Akira stated seriously as he stood at the entryway. "It's
not gonna be easy, not by a long shot, but I know that we can do this."

"But it's dangerous! If we don't pace ourselves--!"

Akira gave Morgana a confident smirk.

"That's what I'm here for. I can go for as long as I need to. We'll make it to the top, even if I
have to drag your asses over the finish line."

Ann leaned close to Ryuji with a curious expression. "He seems...oddly motivated today."
She whispered to him.

"Yeah...think I know why..." Ryuji spoke back in a way that made it obvious that he knew
exactly the reason why. When Ann gave him a questioning look, Ryuji simply said "Tell you
later."

Morgana seemed conflicted. Akira's boldness was admirable, but if his confidence was
misplaced...

But then again, had Akira ever given him a reason to doubt his strength or his judgement?

A minute of hesitation was all that was needed to eventually bring the cat around. "Alright,
fine. We'll try to do this in one go." He sighed. "But if you start to get overwhelmed, we're
turning around, no questions asked. Understand?" Morgana stated with narrowed eyes.

"Don't worry. I'll be careful." Akira replied. He stepped forward, and breathed in silently, his
hand reaching out towards the vile castle. Then he clenched it.

"Okay..." He whispered.

This was it... their first official foray into phantom thievery began now.

"Get ready, gang." Akira grinned to his team.

 

"We're one-shotting this bitch!"

Chapter End Notes



I am really sorry for how long it took this chapter to come out. I have been having
spouts of motivation that were coming and going all through 2024. Some scenes were
hard for me to write, like the Iwai scene for some reason. I genuinely wanna reach the
end of this. There are still so many scenes I want to write. This story means a lot and I
want to finish it, even though in the grand scheme, I've barely begun.

I can't promise a consistent uploading schedule, but I hope you can be patient anyway. I
appreciate each and every one of you for reading this fic and enjoying it and I hope that
I can pick up my pace and start producing chapters faster. A year is really long without
an update.

If nothing else, I'd like to include an omake or two, to give you something in the lulls
between main chapters. It's not much but I hope it can give you some kind of
satisfaction.

Nevertheless, I hope that you enjoyed this chapter and are looking forward to the future
of this fic!



Chapter 8: Omake: Song of the Chariot
Chapter Notes
See the end of the chapter for notes

"So, what was all that about? You and that guy were looking pretty serious." 

Akira shook his head and hefted his bag over his shoulder, now filled with several fake
firearms in addition to a slightly haughty cat, who seemed equally interested in the answer.

"Dude thought I was some punk kid. He told me he wasn't gonna sell me anything." Akira
said as they walked through the back alley. The distant hustle and bustle of Shibuya Central
Street graced their ears. "We sorted it out, though; I got what we needed." He added.

Ryuji paused for a moment, and he peeked back at Untouchable, then back to Akira. "You
didn't rob him, did you?"

The bespectacled teen scoffed. "Oh yes, Ryuji, I totally robbed him, and now you're an
accessory to the crime." He replied with an overly satirical voice.

Ryuji raised his hands defensively. "Hey, man, I was just checkin'! Things just sorta tend
to...get punched around you, and that whole thing felt like one of those times." 

"Nobody got punched," Akira insisted. "We just talked, sorted things out."

He decided not to mention just how close things had actually come to them throwing down.
If it weren't for Uncle Kaz's name, he probably would have.

 

...maybe Ryuji had a point.

 

The blond sighed and relaxed his shoulders. "That's good. Don't think I could deal with any
more shit right now. Don’t wanna get arrested by association on top of that." He half-joked.

Morgana let a cheeky grin crawl across his catty face. "I don't know Ryuji, I think you'd look
right at home behind bars!" He teased.

"Why you little--!" Ryuji snarled. “You wanna go? I’ll throw down with a cat, I don’t give a
crap!”

"Ack! I’m not a cat!" Morgana protested before allowing his face to curl into that smug
expression that all cats seemed to have on standby. “And to do that, you’ll need to go through
this guy, right, Akira?”



Said “this guy” sighed as they stepped out onto Central Street. Honestly, these two goofballs.
Why was he the only one on this team with an ounce of maturity?

He opened his mouth to chastise the pair, but before he could, he found his attention drawn to
something a little ways down the road.

 

Wait...was that a...!?

 

"Ugh..." Ryuji glanced down the street. "Damn cat..."

"I just told you; I'm not a—m-mrow!?"

The sudden stoppage of Morgana’s retort went over the blond’s head. Instead, Ryuji glanced
down the street, his eyes eventually being drawn to the cinema at the end. 

Come to think of it, there was a movie that had recently come out that he'd been interested in
seeing. The Cake Knight Rises. Critics claimed that it was poorly written, but it had a
passionate protagonist fighting for justice, kinda like what they were doing as phantom
thieves.

It occurred to Ryuji that between the palace shenanigans, the conspiracy against Kamoshida
and the stress of the past few days, they hadn't actually had the opportunity to hang out, do
normal dude stuff, like slurping ramen or working out at the gym just down the road. There
was some daylight left, so perhaps they could change that. 

He spun around. "Hey, Akira, y'wanna see a movie while we're...huh?" 

The empty air gave no answer.

Akira was gone. It was weird. Ryuji could have sworn that he was right behind him...

"Akira? Akira!?" He looked around in confusion, seeing nothing but a cluster of people
diving in and out of shops. An old guy walking his dog, a little girl asking her dad for a
crepe, a dude in a suit whose slicked back hair could probably stop a bullet…but among
them, there was no sign of who he was searching for. In a place like Shibuya Central Street, it
was easy to lose someone, and hard to find them, especially with crowds like this. Ryuji
foolishly possessed the notion that spotting Akira would be hard. 

It was not.

It took Ryuji all of five seconds to look down the length of the street and be met with the
highly conspicuous sight of his badass new friend pressing his face against the Pasela Resorts
window, looking more excited than he'd ever seen him. Hell, more excited than he'd ever seen
anyone.

With a questioning stare, Ryuji slowly approached Akira. "Uh, what are you doing?"



And he had to take a step back when Akira turned his head to him with his eyes sparkling
like he was in a freaking shōjo manga.

"Ryuji," he gasped. "They have karaoke here! Karaoke!" 

Oh. Ryuji took a glance inside. Booths in the back with faint singing ranging from passable to
talentless, and a receptionist who was looking at Akira with a hint of fear.

"Do you...like karaoke?" Ryuji asked. It took him a second to realise how dumb of a question
that was, considering Akira's excitement.

Morgana shook his head from the impromptu teleportation and flicked one of his ears. "I
know that everyone's got a hobby, but karaoke? I didn't expect that from you." 

Well, Morgana could blame Kiryu for that. Akira went to one karaoke session with him and
came away with an…

 

Well, 'obsession' was such an ugly word, but not an inaccurate one.

 

"We're going in here." Akira declared.

"Whuh?" Ryuji squawked, but the firm grasp around his wrist indicated that Akira would be
hearing no objections. Hell, Ryuji probably wouldn’t even be able to request a bathroom
break from the guy.

Singing karaoke…it was not how Ryuji expected an outing to stock up on equipment would
end, but...it was nice to learn something about his new friend?

Welp, Cake Knight could wait another day.

(A couple of days later, after a successful infiltration of the Palace.

 

"No, I'm serious Ann! It was crazy as shit!"

"Suuuuure, Ryuji." Ann scoffed while filling in another answer that she was about 43%
confident in at best. There was probably more sound maths going into that estimation than
whatever the heck she had just written down. 

It was lunchtime at Shujin Academy. Akira had gone to the school store to grab a bite,
leaving Ann to be regaled with Ryuji’s crazy story.

She placed her pen down on her desk and rested her cheek in her left hand. She took a
cursory glance around the classroom to make sure nobody was eavesdropping before



continuing in a quiet tone. "Y'know, we literally just got done running through a castle in a
world of cognition, and I still think it sounds nuts." 

"It's true though! Akira pretty much dragged me to the karaoke place in Central Street
because he really, really, really likes karaoke..."

Well, belting it out with some friends with some songs was a great time. Her and Shiho had
gone a few times themselves. Ann could reasonably imagine that Akira, a passionate guy,
would be into something just as passionate, like karaoke. That was the easy-to-believe part.

"..and then, when I started singin', everything changed!" 

"You know, I don't really sing that much, dude. You sure you want me to take the lead here?"
Ryuji glanced at Akira, who was tapping his microphone. 

"You'll do great, Ryuji. Karaoke's all about grabbing the mic and just cutting loose. Taking
everything inside you and belting it out like a goddamn rockstar.” Akira grinned eagerly. “I'll
be backing you up too. Trust me, this is gonna be so much fun!" 

Ryuji couldn't help but chuckle. "Still super weird to see you like this, dude." He spoke while
shaking his head. 

Akira rolled his eyes and gave a snort. "Yeah, yeah, birds are swimming, fish are flying, dogs
and cats living together, now come on, pick a song!" He insisted. 

"Okay, okay! Sheesh..." Ryuji gave a bemused sigh as he glanced to the tablet in front of
them. Millions of song possibilities. What should he choose?

"Um...ooh, this one!" Ryuji pressed on one of the Trending songs. He hit the confirmation
button and their song began to load.

Akira rolled the mic in his hands energetically and looked at their sole spectator. "Ready on
the tambourine, Morgana?" He asked.

"Ready!" The cat announced. He was holding a single tambourine kind of clumsily between
his two hind paws, but he was clearly excited as well. He'd never done karaoke before, or at
least he didn't remember doing it. Either way, it was cute.

The screen before them began to count down, and Ryuji swallowed. Akira would have the
first few lines. He only hoped that their leader didn't prove too hard of an act to follow.

There came the pounding of drums while the speakers came to life. Lyrics appeared on the
screen, and Akira raised the mic to his mouth. 

"After eight years I got the balls to confess but my crush kicked me to the curb!"

 

It was a rich voice filled with vigour that Ryuji was met with. 



"I poured my heart in that love letter!"

"She tore it up, put my feelings through the shredder!"

"Our relationship, it won't get better"

"She left me before I could forget her!"

 

Ryuji sucked in a breath and prepared for his speaking lines.

"So, uh, you know that girl you like's got a boyfriend? I hear he's captain of the soccer team
and hot, and smart, and athletic. He's like the whole package, a perfect ten outta ten, but
you're not in the same league, dude! Hahahaha!"

The words went by kind of fast, but the blond managed to keep up.

Guitar riffs filled the air while Akira balled his fist and pounded his chest.

"It's fine!"

♪♪♪♪

"It's fine!"

♪♪♪♪

"Summertime Groove!"

They entered the main part of the song, and Ryuji furrowed his brow.

"I'm really sorry, I feel bad, I do."

"(Yeah, I went through something awful, y'know?)"

"You had your eyes on her for so long

But everybody else did too!"

"Apparently, those two are going four years strong"

"(Really? Four years? It's been that long?)"

"How can someone be this damn slow?

Even a jerk like you oughta know!"

 



Akira began realistically sobbing into his mic, straight up wailing like he'd actually had his
heart broken. It was honestly kind of hilarious how seriously he was taking this, and Ryuji
fought to stay on track instead of cracking up.

"No need to go and cry. Deep down, you're a good guy."

"(I'm sorry! I'm so sorry!)"

"You need to get your mind off things — I know! How 'bout a trip to Hawaii? Let's go!"

 

And that was when things stopped making sense. 

Because Ryuji suddenly found his vision going white, and when it faded, they weren't in the
karaoke place anymore. Instead, he and Akira found themselves suddenly standing on a
stage, him twirling a beach towel above his head and Akira jumping around and hyping up a
crowd of hundreds. 

And around them? White sand, radiant sunshine, and the most gorgeous blue ocean that Ryuji
had ever seen.

Wait...was this Hawaii? From the song?

 

What the fuck!?

 

He glanced at Akira, who didn't seem perturbed by this sequence of events at all, and then
back to the crowd. They were all twirling their towels above their heads too. And in the
front...was that Morgana in his Metaverse form, sitting on Ann's shoulder, both of them
cheering the performing pair on?

 

How in the hell was this happening?

 

And why was Ryuji compelled to keep singing like this was normal? He had a million
questions, but he...he didn't want to stop! This was insane!

"So grab your towel, and dry off your tears!"

"(This grief ain't ever gonna leave me be!)"

"Now move your feet, just get down and dance!"

"(Well now I'm feelin' that trip to Hawaii!)"



"Summer's here!"

He tossed his towel skyward.

And before Ryuji's eyes, white ensnared his surroundings again, and they found themselves
now on a mostly silent beach, planted on the sand, staring at a brilliant sunset.

Where did the towels go? The crowd? Was he high?

"Let's hit the beach and flirt with some babes!"

"(Think I could pet some doggos too?)"

His befuddlement was broken as Akira frantically tapped on his shoulder and pointed at a
passing woman in a bikini. A stalwart image of mature sexiness. She turned to the two boys,
posed seductively at them and winked. 

"Maybe there's a chance you'll meet your soulmate!"

"(Y'know I'm kinda gettin' into this now!)"

 

And Ryuji started to think, as both he and Akira swooned before the woman, that maybe he
didn't need to have the answers...

"Go on, make your move!"

"Get into that summertime groove!"

(Yeah!)

And in a blink, they were back on stage, dancing around as the returning crowd tossed up
their towels from sheer unrelenting hype. The duo scrambled around the stage with wide
grins coaxing the crowd to dance along; to make some goddamn noise!

"(It's fine!)"

♪♪♪♪

"(It's fine!)"

♪♪♪♪

"(Summertime Groove!)"

Synchronised surfer poses. A perfect ending. Crowds cheering, sun beaming. Ryuji was out
of breath, but he felt incredible. Indescribable. Completely and utterly incognizant, but who
gave a shit?

 



Karaoke with Akira was AWESOME!

"You're saying that singing karaoke with Akira somehow ended with you performing on a
stage in Hawaii complete with cheering crowds and random women trying to flirt with you?"

"L-Look, I know it sounds crazy..."

"What it sounds like is that you had waaaaay too much sugar before bed," Ann muttered as
she popped a stick of pocky in her mouth and nibbled away at it like a beaver.

Coming from Ann, that was saying something. 

"I'm not lying! It felt so real! And I scored a 95 too!" Ryuji insisted. The last time he went, he
hadn't even managed to break a score of 80. It was as if Akira's overflowing energy and
enthusiasm had boosted his singing skills to superhuman levels. 

"Pfft, yeah right!" Ann rolled her eyes. Even her personal best was only 92. No way was
Ryuji better at singing than her. That was even harder to believe than this whole Hawaii mini-
musical-moment they apparently had was.

 

...

 

Although...there was a small grain of doubt that managed to work its way into her mind.
Considering what she'd experienced during the past week, could she really rule it out?

99% of her said that she could. But there was that one percent that was aware that this was
Akira they were talking about. And the things that he could do, both in the Metaverse and out
of it defied explanation.

God, she had exams to study for, a treasure to steal; did she really have the luxury to humour
this wild tale of Ryuji's?

Well, from the sound of it, Akira was super fond of karaoke, and she had been trying to find
ways to make it up to him for everything that he'd done for her and Shiho. A karaoke outing
would probably be refreshing for them. 

She'd satiate her curiosity, prove Ryuji wrong and treat Akira all in one go. 

And conveniently enough, it was that moment that Akira chose to walk back into the
classroom, munching on some jam bread from the school store. He beelined to the two
blondes while ignoring any wary stares from his peers. He'd gotten unfortunately adept at
weathering the attention.

He sat down on his chair between the pair, who looked to be in a kind of standoff. He
swallowed a mouthful of bread and looked between them with perplexity. "Did I...miss



something?" He asked. 

Ann shook her head. "No, not really," she answered. "More importantly," she leaned forward,
"after we deal with Kamoshida, would you maybe like to...do some karaoke together?"

"Would I!" 

And with a face full of a starry-eyed Akira, Ann had the vaguest sense of stepping into a
realm she probably should have left well alone...

 

And so shattered the first of seven seals...

 

Chapter End Notes

Hey, so a couple of things have happened recently. Firstly, I've just moved out of my
parents' house, so I'm trying to get used to a new environment. It's gonna be a bit weird

I have been working on Chapter 8 proper, (Which this omake technically happens in the
middle of, but I wanted to write it so deal with it.) but I figured that it would be nice to

The second thing is something that I am genuinely astounded by: This fic has a

Chapter 9: Battle of the Castle of Lust Part 1
Chapter Notes
See the end of the chapter for notes

"We one-shot that bitch!"

Akira was feeling very fulfilled as he let himself fall onto one of the sofas of the final Safe
Room. The climb to the top of this rancid castle had been beyond treacherous. Shadows
lurked around every corner, and as they neared the top, the very layout shifted around them
like stretching putty. Pillars rose, stairs appeared out of nowhere, and the security grew ever
tighter

Not only that, but this place really tried hard to maintain the vibe of a mysterious castle by
throwing puzzles at them. Like the chains they had to pull in a particular order to gain access
to a map of the Palace, or the library with the books they had to re-shelve.

That latter one was Akira's favourite. The shelves were stuffed to the brim with books, each
one with a title that correlated to the figures in Kamoshida's life. Like the Slave Book that
matched to a section on male students that Kamoshida considered under him. The shelves
went on and on... 

'Ryuji Sakamoto: The Vulgar Ape', 'Mishima Yuuki: The Snivelling No-Hoper.' Even the
principal was in there, under the title of 'Principal Kobayakawa: The Pudgy Pushover'.

Then there was the King Book that obviously went in the section that spoke of Kamoshida's
general greatness and his exploits. His numerous victories on the court, the medals he won...

...the thorns he had removed...

But there was a special book thrown behind one of the bookcases, one that Akira only spotted
thanks to his Third Eye. It had a battered cover and a spine that was almost falling apart, as if
it had been kicked around, stomped on, and thrown against any wall in sight. It was called the
Foil Book. And all that was inside were maddened scrawlings of how much Kamoshida hated
Akira.

He hated his smugness, his prowess at volleyball, his rebelliousness, even his glasses. Every
conceivable thing there was to hate about a person was written in this book. The contempt
was practically woven into the ink.

Very pleasant read, all things considered. Akira highly recommended it.

It was slotted into its own section, a bookcase with similarly battered books, their damage
relative to Kamoshida's grievances towards the subjects that said books held. Akira couldn't
help but feel proud at his own book's damage compared to the others. A pride not dissimilar
to a craftsman with weathered tools.



That aside, they hadn't gone completely unopposed as they made their way through the
ostentatious halls and hopped across golden chandeliers probably worth more than several
sports cars. There were Shadows to fight almost every step of the way, and some of them
hadn't gone down easy, like that weird metal angel that had stopped them in the church. It
was certainly a cut above the other Shadows but it soon fell, like all the others. 

The team had been very thorough during the infiltration. No chest left unopened, no Shadow
left unextorted for money, items or their fealty. Every battle they went through made them
stronger, and every victory brought them closer to their prize.

And fattened their wallets. Don't forget that part.

In any case, after climbing the castle's highest tower and locating Kamoshida's throne room,
it was a simple matter of taking the upper walkway to sneak above the final Shadows and
find the treasure room behind it.

And as they cracked the lock and pushed the doors open, atop a massive pile of coins, jewels
and oversized trophies sat their prize. The Palace's Treasure. 

Sort of. 

It was a formless, wavy blob that floated in the middle of the room and didn't really look like
anything, but Morgana insisted that this was indeed the treasure they had been searching for.
Finding it had only been the first step, and the second was one that Morgana had already
explained to Akira in great detail. 

Send Kamoshida a calling card in the real world, like proper thieves, and word it in a way
that indicated that his desires were not only tangible, but stealable. That would be what
would turn this blob into the treasure they sought. Remove it from the Palace and they were
home free. Hopefully.

With the route secured, the team had doubled back to the closest Safe Room to rest before
exfiltrating. They had done it in one clean run, but Ann and Ryuji were definitely running on
fumes. Ryuji had collapsed on one of the opposite couches. Ann didn't even make it that far
and fell to the floor at her blond teammate's feet.

"Skull," Ann groaned out, sliding her mask off of her face. "We got...any water left?" She
asked. 

"Ugh...lemme check..." Ryuji took a moment to check the party's pack and eventually
withdrew a bottle of water. "Yeah, we got some."

"Good." Ann gave a small nod. "Now drown me."

It was a testament to Ryuji's own tiredness that he didn't question Ann's order, uncapped the
bottle and tipped out the water onto her face. And all that the girl could respond with was a
faint "Blllbbllbblllbbll..." 



She was soon lying in a chilly puddle on the tiled floor. A bottle of water would have
normally reached room temperature by now, but the Metaverse had a way of ignoring the
laws of thermal equilibrium on whatever they brought in. A fact that Ann was quite thankful
for.

Morgana had taken a seat besides Akira, lulling his head back into the soft backrest. He had
spent most of his energy too, just to keep up with their leader. "Holy crap, we actually did the
whole thing in one go. That's crazy..." He mumbled. 

He heard Akira chuckle and then felt him nudge his side with his hand. "Told you we could
do it!" He exclaimed. 

The boy looked over to the exhausted pair sitting across from them and he gave them a nod.
"You guys did fantastic!" He told them. Ann and Ryuji in particular had fought wonderfully,
considering that they were complete novices when it came to combat. They had given it
everything they had, up until they could finally collapse in the final Safe Room.

He did receive some slightly bitter groans from the blonde duo. Akira was sure that he was
going to be getting some glares from Ann in class tomorrow...

The teen crossed his arms and glanced back to Morgana. "So, Mona, we've secured the
infiltration route, now it's time to make a calling card, right?"

Ryuji blinked and looked at Morgana with curious expression. "A calling-whatsitnow?"

"A calling card," Morgana answered. The not-cat was about to go into an explanation before
Akira placed his hand on his hand, which resulted in a delightful mew, and then two seconds
later, a flustered squawk. "H-Hey! What's that for!?" Morgana hissed.

"Let's save that for now." Akira insisted while glancing to the other two. "We're wiped, and
I'd prefer to discuss the plan when Panther isn't trying to set my hair on fire with her mind."

"Agiiiiiiiii...!" Ann growled.

"Hell you mean "we're wiped?" You're fresh as an 'effing daisy!" Ryuji jabbed.

"It's a royal we!"

"I'm too tired to know what that means."

"You wouldn't know it if you were 100%, Skull~" Morgana cheerfully jabbed before letting
out a sigh. "You make a good point, Joker. We've secured the infiltration route, that's what
matters. We can take a few days to rest up before moving to the next phase of the plan." 

"Plus, we got a whole lotta cool stuff too!" Akira grinned at the ceiling. "Money, items, new
Personas..." He perched one leg over the other. "Yup, we've had a preee-tty good haul today."

"Joker...you look like a mob boss." Morgana deadpanned.

Akira rolled his eyes. Mob bosses wished they could be as cool as this. 



Akira let his tone become a bit more serious. He leaned forward and rested his arms on his
lap. "Remember though, this was only the first step. We still have to actually steal the
treasure, and I guarantee that Kamoshida isn't just gonna let us walk out of here with it. The
hardest, and most dangerous part, is yet to come." 

Akira laced his hands together. "We'll take a few days off to recover, be at our A-game before
we make a move on the treasure. I'll send the details of what we need to do to the group chat
tonight. If either of you have any questions, just ask me...and I'll defer to Mona." 

"What am I, an FAQ booklet?"

Ryuji groaned. "Uuuuugh, I don't wanna think about how sore I'm gonna be tomorrow." 

"Forget tomorrow," Ann mumbled. "I don't even know how I'm gonna get home tonight.
Shiho's going to ask questions..."

"Come on. You guys are all the stronger for it, and in a few days, you'll be glad you did it."
Akira assured them. 

"But everything hurts noooooow." Ann whined.

Akira gave a sigh and dug into his pack. He might have pushed them too far this time, but
hey, he was a novice leader just like they were fighters. They were all learning here. 

Nevertheless, he sympathised that their journey home was going to be a problem for them. In
that case there was only one thing for it.

Substance abuse!

Akira smiled widely while retrieving retrieved two small bottles.

"Who wants Tauriners?"

Akira and Morgana had arrived home just as Sojiro was starting to close up. After getting the
usual stink-eye and a reminder of how thin the ice he was treading on was, the man let Akira
go up to his room.

Just as Sojiro was about to lock up for the night, he thought it prudent to inform Akira that
there was curry laid out for him (because God forbid the kid pulled that hunger strike shit
again) and ascended Leblanc's stairs to inform him.

He had expected to maybe see Akira writing in his diary, studying in bed or something stupid
like doing pull-ups from the rafters. What he didn't expect was to see Akira sitting in the
center of the room with a random cat, whom he was feeding with a packet of beef jerky.

Sojiro had looked at the pair, who stared back at him like deer caught in headlights. The
exchange lasted for all of ten seconds before he pinched the bridge of his nose in
exasperation. 



"Kid...no."

"His name is Morgana...?"

For the love of...he already named it!?

"You can't keep a cat in here. I'm in the hospitality industry! I can't let an animal roam around
the cafe!"

"H-He won't roam! He's very well behaved, and I'll keep him up here when you have
customers!" Akira protested.

Sojiro groaned. He’d expected this kid to be a handful when he agreed to take him in, but
seriously!? He should have negotiated for a steeper pay...

"Where'd you even find him?" Sojiro asked.

"I... I was walking through Central Shibuya, and I saw a few brats throwing rocks at him."
Akira reached out and picked up Morgana, who gave a startled yowl at the sudden
manhandling. "I scared those little pricks away. Luckily, this little guy wasn't hurt too bad.
He's a toughie, this one."

He stroked the cat’s sleek black fur, coaxing a low purr. "He was pretty calm around me, and
he's smart, so he's probably had owners before. But he didn't have any tags or anything and
he was hanging out in an alley. I think he was abandoned." 

Sojiro's glare faltered a little. A cat that had been discarded by its owners and forced to live
on the street, to be picked on by witless punks who thought that the suffering of a defenceless
animal was funny. It was a story that plucked the strings of his godforsaken bleeding heart. 

“Abandoned, huh?” the man muttered, with a soft voice. “That’s... yeah. That’s a shame.”

He stroked his goatee and closed his eyes. He considered his options. The sensible thing to do
would just be to throw the cat out...but that really wouldn't sit right with him. And it appeared
to be every bit as well behaved as Akira had indicated.

"Christ, kid. Do you even know how to take care of a cat?" Sojiro asked. 

“I used to have a dog. Can’t be that different,” Akira offered.

"Hm... cats and dogs are different, you know." Sojiro hummed in response. Still, that was a
better answer than he'd hoped. Akira having prior experience with a pet would put him a bit
more at ease. The man looked up to the ceiling and watched a tiny speck of dust fall from the
rafters. "Well...I suppose taking care of a pet will help you keep your nose clean...." 

Dammit.

“Fine,” he said at last. “You can keep him. But he’s your responsibility. I’m not feeding him,
I'm not watching him, and he stays upstairs during business hours. If he bothers my
customers, he’s gone. Got it?”



Akira seemed more than happy with the terms, and he nodded his head. "I got it. Thanks,
Sakura--er, Boss." He corrected his slip of the tongue and looked down at the feline. "Say
thanks, Morgana!"

"Mrow!" 

Huh, Sojiro could definitely see why Akira thought the cat was smart. There was a strange
intelligence he could detect from behind those bright blue eyes. It was a little eerie how
aware the cat seemed of the situation actually...

But as long as Akira kept the cat quiet and in line, he'd have no reason to complain. 

And to be honest, how could he with that cute widdle face...?

So, Leblanc officially had a cat now. Neato.

The next order of business was actually catching Ann and Ryuji up to speed. Akira had been
courteous enough to not pile on the necessary information while they were running on fumes
and Tauriners. But ensuring that they knew what Morgana had taught him was of the highest
priority to ensure the success of the coming heist. And that needed to happen sooner rather
than later.

Akira: Hey, if you two are feeling up to it, I'd like to talk about our next steps.

He had sent that text to the group chat, initially believing that he'd need to wait a while to
hear back from either, but he was surprised to receive correspondence from both of them
within a couple of minutes.

Ryuji: 'sup? I can talk.

Ann: Just make it quick, please. I wanna sleep... I already got chewed out by Shiho.

Ryuji: Sounds shit. You didn't tell her anything, right? 

Ann: No, but she's getting suspicious about what I'm getting into. And I'm not good at lying
to her.

Ryuji: Ann, I've known you since middle school. You're not good at lying to anyone.

Akira had known Ann for only a handful of days, but that seemed to check out. Ann was a
girl that wore her heart on her sleeve.

Ann: That's uncalled for... Akira, defend me...

Ryuji: Defend yourself.

Ann: But I'm tiiiiired! 

Akira: Nope, Ryuji's right. You gotta defend yourself.



Ann: Aw, man (¬､¬)

Akira had to keep Morgana from seizing the phone himself to secondhandedly take back that
comment. Not that he would've had much success with his toe beans anyway.

Ann: But seriously, I'm not sure how much longer I can keep this from her. She's getting more
and more anxious. I told her that she doesn't have to worry about Kamoshida anymore, but...

Ryuji: I getcha. After all she's been through, she probably thinks that the bastard's just lying
in wait so he can try again when the heat dies down. Ain't anyone in the volleyball team he
hasn't scared into thinking that...

Akira could sense Ryuji's bitterness through the screen. 

Akira: It's time for that to change. We're stealing that slimeball's heart, or we'll die trying.

Ann: I would very much like to not die!

Ryuji: So then, what's our next move, Akira?

It took the teen a minute to write it all down, but he soon cobbled together a simplified set of
instructions, based on what Morgana had told him. Send a calling card in reality, infiltrate the
palace, steal the materialised treasure and get the hell out before they found themselves on
the wrong end of a knight's sword. 

It took a few seconds for the first reaction to come through.

Ryuji: Cooool! That's like, proper, super-thief stuff! Can I write the calling card? I wanna
tear that shitbag a new one!

And soon after, Ann responded.

Ann: That seems like a pretty important job. Are you sure you're up for it? If I understand
right, he needs to believe that his desires are a thing that can be stolen. If it comes across as
a weirdo's rambling, wouldn't he ignore it? And then his treasure won't appear?

Akira is typing...

Akira: Morgana says that Ann's right. The card needs to have a visceral reaction within the
cognition of the person. A scathing slog of insults are gonna go right over his head. 

Ryuji: I know that! The focus has gotta be on the heart stealing. But can't I let him know how
much of a bastard he is too? He deserves it!

Akira: Of course he does, but we can't afford to mess this up. Once we send it, there’s no
take-backs, no second chances. We can't get so caught up that we miss the point of sending
the calling card in the first place. 

Ryuji: ...just a little bit then?



Akira: Just a little. I'm not heartless.

Ann: I've got an idea. How about we get together tomorrow and we can draft some ideas?
Three heads are better than one. I bet we could come up with something brilliant!

Ann: *I meant four. I forgot about Morgana, sorry (^_ _)人

"Come on, Lady Ann, I'm not a footnote!" Morgana gave an indignant mewl. It made Akira
roll his eyes fondly. He could almost see the hearts floating up from the cat whenever Ann's
name was uttered...

Akira: Brainstorming sounds great. School roof at lunchtime works for everyone?

Ann: Works for me! 

Ryuji: Yeah, I'll be there. I actually just thought of a logo for us. I'm gonna draw it now
before I forget. 

Ann: Do we even need a logo?

Ryuji: Come on, we gotta have a logo! And a name! What's our name gonna be!?

Akira: Save it for tomorrow. Try to get some sleep for now, you guys need it after today.
Joker's orders.

Ann: Wow, what a slave driver XD I'll see you guys tomorrow then. Goodnight.

Ryuji: Imma make that logo before I hit the hay. I'm so freaking pumped! See you later!

Akira sighed and let the phone fall to his side. The vibrations that had plagued the device
from the numerous messages they'd sent each other finally fell silent. It was a little
hyperbolic to say that the silence was deafening, but it was certainly a little jarring to go from
texting so fervently to doing nothing at all. It made him feel a little restless.

So, as he was getting into the habit of doing, he turned to his lone furry companion to fill the
quiet. 

"Hey Morgana?"

The cat preened his head up a little too quickly, as if he'd also been waiting for one of them to
break the silence. "Yeah?" He asked. 

Akira quickly came to the realisation that he didn't actually have a topic to talk about, so he
just said the first thing that came to mind.

"Well I... I just want to thank you." 

"Eh?"



Akira kept on talking, the words coming into his mind as fast as he could speak them. "I
mean, you're the only reason why we're doing what we are. The only reason why there's a
light at the end of this crazy path we started. Before, we had no way out. Nothing concrete
anyway. This was a problem I couldn't punch away. But this; infiltrating a castle, dethroning
a king and stealing his treasure..." Akira glanced at Morgana. "It's a way out of this mess. A
piece of hope that you gave us. And I never actually thanked you for it. So... thanks."

Morgana couldn't even begin to fathom how to reply to that. Until now everything had been
business and the occasional joke at his expense. But to be openly acknowledged and thanked
for what he'd done...

"Um...y-you're welcome?" Morgana replied, turning his head to the side. What was going on?
Akira was supposed to be a cool badass. What was with this sappy stuff?!

...And what was this feeling in his chest when Akira mentioned that he gave them hope? Was
it pride? Embarrassment? No, it was something different. A longing...A familiarity that he
couldn't put into words...

 

"▅o▅...▅▅e...▅▅m▅n▅t▅'s h▅▅▅"

 

What the hell was this weird sense of Deja vu he was feeling? 

 

Hope...

 

Hope...

 

Hope...

 

Why was a single word so significant to him? When all other memories were seemingly lost
to the wind, why did that word resonate with him so? 

Argh! Every time he thought about it, the answer slipped away. He couldn't even get it on the
tip of his tongue!

"Hm...? You good Morgana? You've got a weird look on your face." Akira commented,
which brought the cat out of his musing.



"Y-Yeah. I was just thinking about something. Don't worry about it." Morgana replied
somewhat mutely while resting his head down on the thin mattress. 

"You sure? I'm happy lending an ear. Don't have much else to do right now." Akira
answered. 

Morgana sighed. "What we should be doing is trying to get some sleep. We had an insane day
today." 

"And an insane haul." Akira grinned as he pat the mattress, a gesture to the duffle bag hidden
beneath, containing all of their technically-ill-gotten-but-also-technically-completely-legal-
gains. "Those golden Kamoshida busts alone have got to be worth some serious cash. I mean,
a golden piece of trash is still gold, right? I wonder if I could smelt 'em to increase their
value..."

"Smelt them? I think you might be getting carried away. We might be better off finding a
fence or something. Get a smaller cut in exchange for keeping things under the radar."
Morgana proposed. 

"Ugh...Iwai mentioned something like that, but he'd take a hefty commission for that, and I
don't wanna settle for a fourth of the price. It's extortion, I tell you."

"Akira, today you held a Shadow out of a window and didn't let them back in until they gave
you all they had." Morgana deadpanned. "I swear, you're too obsessed with money..."

Akira grumbled in reply. Spoken like someone who didn't owe five million yen to a scumbag
by year's end. What he wouldn't give to be able to steal that bastard's heart...

"Come on," Morgana mewled. "You're doing enough as it is. You've secured gear and
medicine for us, you're crazy good in battle, and you're a great leader!"

"Did we ever actually elect me as leader?"

"You're the obvious choice!"

Akira chuckled and placed his hands behind his head. "You sure? I mean, you're the
Metaverse expert. I was just winging it and punching whatever got in my way the first time.
It's only when you showed up that we had a clue what we were doing." He said.

The cat shook his head fondly. "Being a leader is about more than just knowing stuff. It's
about rallying people around you, staying calm under pressure, helping a team become more
than the sum of its parts, and..." 

 

"I know that he saved our lives, but we don't have a choice. The only thing we can do
is...make sure he doesn't die for nothing."

 



Morgana paused for a second, turning his head so that Akira couldn't spot the guilt in his
eyes.

"...and making sure everyone gets back home alive." He concluded.

This was a guy who dove into the Metaverse to save his friends despite not having a Persona,
who released him from his captivity when the others were debating on freeing him or not,
who was willing to continue fighting alongside Morgana, even after Kamoshida was dealt
with.

"And you were more than willing to sacrifice him if it meant saving yourself." A traitorous
voice rumbled in his head.

No... he could never be the leader if those were the kinds of decisions he made.

Akira stared at Morgana for a few seconds. He seemed to be weighing those words—that
faith—in his head for a moment, before he finally nodded in acceptance. "Well, I'll try not to
disappoint, I guess." He replied with a slightly awkward smile.

...oh great, and now Morgana was feeling too guilty to sleep. He needed a distraction.

He stole a glance at Akira, who had just plugged in his phone to charge. "Hey… what time is
it?" Morgana asked quietly,

Akira reopened his phone to check. "About 8:26. Why?"

Hm, was it too late?

Nah. 

Morgana got to his feet (paws?) and met Akira's curious stare. "Get out of bed," he ordered.
"I'm teaching you how to make lockpicks."

"Huh?"

And that was how Akira found himself sitting at the workbench in the corner of his room,
fiddling with some of the random scraps of metal that they had acquired that day. Morgana
had gone over the steps with him during the following hour, and as expected, Akira learned
quickly. 

Then another ten minutes passed and Morgana was staring, jaw agape as Akira's hands
moved in a refined flurry while his stare was laser-focused. Morgana couldn't even see the
crafting materials under the blur of motion. 

Then, with not an ounce of drama spared, Akira slashed his pliers outwards as if he were a
samurai, slicing through an enemy ronin. His brow furrowed, his teeth bared and his created
lockpick somehow gleaming with a golden light.

The noise that Morgana let out could charitably be compared to that of a strangled parrot.



"Nothing we used was golden! What the hell?!"

The bulletin board in the middle of Shujin's hallway was unimportant on the best of days. It
had numerous flyers for different clubs, and weathered notices of school events long since
forgotten to both the students and faculty. 

But today, it was different. That board was the talk of the school, unmistakably due to the
numerous red and black cards haphazardly bespeckling its surface. The words 'TAKE YOUR
HEART' were emblazoned on the front with scratchy font, beneath a shadowy, shark-toothed
figure in a top hat.

Curious students crowded around the board, each drawn to the mysterious cards, and the
ominous content scribed onto them.

 

"Sir Suguru Kamoshida, the sinner of lust whose spotlight has long since faded. You, who
this world trusts to guide the next generation, have instead used your authority to bully

those who cannot fight back, to rob futures and innocence alike. We will not stand for this.
We will make you confess your crimes with your own mouth. We are the Phantom Thieves

of Hearts, and your twisted desires are ours to take."

 

"Whaddya think? These babies whipped up quite a stir, huh?"

Akira lowered the card he'd snatched from the board to look at Ryuji, who was giving him a
thousand-megawatt grin. He was the one who insisted on posting the cards, and Akira had to
admit that he'd done a great job. 

"Yeah," Akira nodded. "There's no way that Kamoshida can ignore being called out like
this." 

He turned his ear to the cluster of peers. He could hear the occasional "A calling card?" or "I
heard it was already posted by the time everyone got here this morning." but the rest was
mostly lost in the clamour. 

"How did you pin these up without getting caught anyway?" Akira asked. 

"Heh!" Ryuji winked and scrubbed the tip of his nose with his finger. "You shoulda seen me,
man. I was like a ninja!" He spoke with unbridled cockiness. 

Said cockiness was swiftly diminished as Morgana surfaced from Akira's bag. "Ryuji, I kept
an eye on you. You were crawling around on the floor in a hoodie humming spy music."

"Wha--! Y-You were spying' on me?! Damn cat!" 

"Well, I had to make sure you didn't screw it up! And I told you, I'm NOT a cat!"



Thankfully, before Ryuji and Morgana's scuffle could get any more heated, Ann jogged up to
them with a giddy expression. "The cards came out awesome, didn't they?" She spoke
enthusiastically. Then her glace took on a somewhat sly tone as she looked at Akira. "I mean,
we could have done a better job on the logo, buuuuut..."

Akira could feel the stares of the two blondes and cat dig into him. He let out a sigh. "There
was no reason to include a dragon in the logo. They have nothing to do with thief stuff."

"But dragons are cool!" Ryuji protested. "And you've got the whole motif going for you! The
dragon on your back, you're strong like one, not to mention the fact that you're an 'effin
dragon in Kamoshida's--"

"Who's responsible for this!?"

Ah, speak of the devil and he shall limp onto the scene. 

Kamoshida had returned to school not long after they'd cleared the Palace, but his
confrontation with Akira had left him worse for wear and unable to perform his so-called
"duties". A mercy for everyone involved. 

The fight had left the athlete bruised all over, blotches of purple and brown dotting his body
while a cooling compress was taped to his cheek. Of course, the rumours were flying around
as to how he ended up in that condition, but a stern scowl from him silenced any attempts to
get more information. The leading theory was that he got mugged, which worked well
enough for Akira. 

Even battered, the man had a presence that sent the students scrambling away as he started
frantically throwing accusations around. "Was it you?!" here and "Or was it you?!" there.

"Heh, isn't this peachy?" Morgana's tail flicked in satisfaction. "His twisted desires are called
out, and he immediately knows what we mean. A reaction like that will definitely cause the
treasure to appear." 

As the students cleared out, Kamoshida turned around, his hard gaze soon being drawn to
them. And it was fascinating to watch how quickly his blotchy face paled once he realised
who he was staring at.

Akira furrowed his brow and cracked his knuckles.

And Kamoshida used every ounce of his training and physique to limp away in the opposite
direction.

"Dayum! Kamoshida's friggin' terrified of you!" Ryuji exclaimed, his expression that of
vindictive glee. 

"He'd better be scared." Akira scoffed while placing a hand on his hip. "And if he isn't, there
are about fifteen things in this hallway I can use to remind him why he should be." He
pointed. "Those chairs, that fire extinguisher, Ann's heels."

"You are NOT using my shoes to bludgeon Kamoshida... You'd ruin them!" Ann protested.



The half-joke hung in the air for a second before the quartet cracked up in stymied laughter,
even Ann, who hadn't even intended for it to be funny. It would probably have been a weird
site if the halls weren't empty. 

They calmed down a few moments later, and Ann's smile downturned into a grimace. "That
bastard finally knows what it's like to be one of his students. Hurt. Scared. Unable to get
help...I still think it's too good for him though." She snarled. 

Akira placed his hand on the blonde's shoulder and gave it a comforting squeeze. "It'll be all
over soon. We'll steal his treasure and make him confess to everything. Shiho can come back
to school, and you'll never have to look at his disgusting face ever again."

"Remember though," Morgana interjected with a firm voice, "we've only got one shot at this.
We have to steal the treasure today. We'll get no second chances."

"That's fine..." Akira replied. His eyes traced over each of them. His friends. 

Ryuji, fists balled and jaw clenched, sparks dancing in his eyes. Ann, breathing deep,
drawing strength from every rise and fall of her chest. Morgana, fur bristled, gaze steady and
sharp.

Akira let a subtle grin creep at the corners of his mouth. 

"...Because one is all we'll need.

It was honestly a little anticlimactic afterwards, since they all had to sit through the remaining
school day. It was kind of hard to focus on calculus and upcoming midterms when they had a
goddamn heist to pull that afternoon.

They were all twitchy, ready for action. Ann dropped her pen several times from fiddling
with it too much and Akira couldn't stop his leg from bouncing. 

When the final bell rung, both of them practically exploded out of their seats, to meet in the
alleyway across from Shujin that had become their infiltration point. Ryuji met them there
shortly afterwards.

And from there, the heist was a go.

The Palace security level had risen to its maximum. The air felt more oppressive and it
buzzed angrily against their skin like it was trying to force them out. But the group of thieves
would sound no retreat. They would persevere. They'd come too far to turn back now. 

Their already established infiltration route ensured an easy path to the treasure. They didn't
have to fight a single Shadow, a luxury that lasted all the way to the treasure room. The
imposing double doors stood before them, yet still the team did not falter, and pushed them
open to reveal their prize.

And if there were any remaining doubts that the calling card hadn't worked, they were
immediately put to rest by the massive royal crown hovering in the air, dotted with jewels



and gold bands. If anything in this place screamed "steal me" it was definitely this. 

Morgana certainly seemed convinced that it was the treasure anyway, from the way that he
threw himself at the crown and snuggled it like it was a gigantic bag of catnip. 

"Mrrrrroooooow!" He purred loudly, clamping all four paws around the crown’s base in a
full-body embrace, his tail swishing with excitement.

His sudden bizarre behaviour gave the three students pause.

"Uh...the cat's actin' weird." Ryuji stated, as if that wasn't obvious enough. Their guide of
hitherto sound judgement and mind was now rubbing his face against the shiny crown like a
man—or rather, a cat—possessed.

"Meow! Meow! Mewwwww~!"

Ryuji looked over at Ann, who obviously had no idea what was going on either. She crossed
her arms in utter confusion. “Has he ever been this…uh, catty before?” she asked, turning to
Akira...

...who was frantically stabbing at the camera app on his phone to try and immortalise this
moment.

"Dude, seriously?" 

"Look at him!" Akira whined "He's adorable!" 

"Meeeeeooooooowwwww~!"

Unfortunately, pressing on the camera app 573 times made no difference to just pressing it
once and with the importance of the mission taking precedence, Akira had to reluctantly
pocket his phone. 

Next time: polaroid. 

Ryuji shook his head and sauntered up to Morgana. "Alright, that's enough, you stupid cat."
He reached out to grab Morgana by the scruff...

"HIIIIISSSSS!"

The blond let out a yelp and fell onto his butt as Mona absconded one paw from the crown to
try and claw at his hand. Blue, unfocused eyes stared at Ryuji for a few seconds. Unblinking.
Hard. Then, like nothing ever happened, Morgana went back to purring and crown snuggling.

"Th-The hell's the matter with you? You feral or something!?" Ryuji squeaked while getting
to his feet. 

"Is it possible that the treasure is affecting him somehow? Is that what twisted desires do?"
Ann asked. 



"I mean, it didn't affect any of us." Akira reasoned. "It's probably specific to Mona." 

"Anyway, someone else has got to get him, cuz I'm not getting my face clawed off." Ryuji
huffed. "Panther, he probably won't claw you. You get him."

"What!? Hell no! I don't want to get clawed either!" Ann protested. 

"Well, we can't carry the damn thing outta here with him swiping at us!" Ryuji retorted. 

Both parties then went silent and looked at Akira, who blinked, then sighed. Of course...

He slowly approached the euphoric cat, raising his arms up. "Morgana, if you can hear me,
I'm just going to pick you up. Everything's gonna be okay." He told the cat with a soft voice.
He didn't get a response, but didn't get swiped and hissed at like Ryuji either, so that was a
definite improvement. 

Akira was able to gently loop an arm around Morgana's body and used the other to stroke his
furry head. The past few days had taught Akira of that sweet spot and he was more than
happy to abuse it. 

"Meeeeeewww~" Morgana let out another purr, taking to the petting immediately. His grip on
the crown started to slacken and gradually, one by one, Akira pried each paw from the crown,
all while whispering soothing things in his ear like "I've got you" and "You're going to be
alright."

There may also have been some "Pspspsps..."'s

It was only when Morgana was completely separated from the crown that his clarity finally
returned. And with that clarity, the cat realised that he'd made an utter fool of himself and he
was currently being cradled by the team badass. 

Needless to say, he was completely mortified. Especially since Akira was still petting him!

His white fur did nothing to hide the red that filled his cheeks, and all he could do was kick
his way out of Akira's clutches and jump to the floor before the (admittedly deluxe) petting
put him in a second trance. 

"Uhm...A-Ahem... sorry about that. I don’t know what came over me," Morgana muttered,
his ears flattening in embarrassment as the coin-littered floor suddenly became the most
interesting thing in the room. Where was a hole to crawl in when he needed one...?

"You feeling alright now?" Akira questioned.

"Yeah...it was just...all of a sudden, I couldn't think. I wasn't prepared and it just...drew me in.
Human desires are something else..." Morgana answered with a little shiver 

"Well, do you think that it'll happen again? We can't have you lashing out like that." Ann
pressed.



Morgana shrunk back a little and he shook his head. "No, no! It was a one-time thing, I
swear! I’ve got it under control now." He gave the team an awkward thumbs up, as if that
would add credibility.

Ryuji stepped forward. "It better be. You were this close to slashing my face!" He informed,
pincering his thumb and forefinger.

Morgana huffed in reply. "Hmpf! Well it's not like I was damaging anything important, was
I?"

"What'd you say?!"

"Alright, break it up, you two." Ann stepped in between them before an actual fight could
break out. "Let's focus on the task at hand, please?" 

Ryuji ruffled his hair in frustration. "Haaah, you're right...but still, look at this thing!" He
approached the crown and looked it up and down. "It's got to weigh a ton. How're we gonna
lug it out of here?" 

"We might not have to." 

The trio looked at Akira, who walked past the crown and rested his hands on the bars of the
treasure room's window. They were thicker than anything they had seen thus far. A quick
wiggle confirmed their sturdiness, as well as Akira's thought process.

He turned to the team with a grin. "Panther. I'm going to need fire. And Skull, I'll need a
Tarukaja..."

 

The dragon on his back flashed yellow...

The throne room was flooded with tension as Shadow Kamoshida’s ground his teeth in fury.
He sat upon his throne, one hand clutching a volleyball tight enough to make the leather
squeak beneath his grip, and the other wrapped around his princess, who answered with
nothing but airheaded moans.

This throne room would be the stage for his brilliant ambush. He was beyond furious that
these thieves had got as far as his treasure room. They were probably getting their filthy mitts
on his greatest treasure right now.  

But it would be a necessary sacrifice for the roaches to deliver themselves right into his
hands. There was no other way out of that room, and the crown was unwieldy in anyone's
hands other than his. They would come this way and he would dispose of them. 

Every muscle in his body was tense in anger. The sheer outrage he felt from seeing his
authority publicly challenged on the bulletin board could melt an iceberg. The halls crawling
with gossip and whispers, students staring at his bandaged face with poorly veiled curiosity...



Each inconvenience made the very walls of his castle feel thinner, more vulnerable. His hard-
won lifestyle was being stripped away by the day. By the dragon and by these damned
thieves. 

But all that was about to change. He would crush those pesky rats into a bloody paste, and
everything would be right again. He'd stand unopposed once again. He would be the king
again...

...but what on earth was taking those thieves so long? They should have been here by now...

His teeth ground in impatience, counting the seconds it took for his prey to show. Any
moment now. They'd be here any moment now...

 

KER-CHUNK!

 

That awful sound of metal being torn away, as well as the way the throne room shook slightly
had Kamoshida's stomach lodging in his throat. Something was wrong. Something was very
seriously wrong!

His ambush plan quickly forgotten, Kamoshida sprang up from his throne and sprinted over
to his treasure room. But a part of him already knew that it was too late. 

As he shoved the double doors open, he was met with a room devoid of thieves, devoid of his
precious crown...and devoid of the bars that were supposed to be blocking off the window.

Kamoshida gaped at the scene. His brain tried to process the fact that the thieves had
outmaneuvered him once again. But in the end, it could only produce a single word.

 

"FUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUU--!!!"

Chapter End Notes

Well, finally another main chapter out. I hope that you considered it a nice read. If you
think that it was a bit abrupt, then you'd think right, since I decided to split the chapter
before the Kamoshida fight in the interests of getting a chapter out at all.

I look back at the first year of this fanfic and I am amazed at how much progress I was
able to make, but as I said before, my motivation is not what it used to be. I could be
inert for five weeks, and then suddenly decide to write 400 words, and that's how it's
been recently. I'm sorry if that makes the chapters seem rushed. In my head, I want the



chapters to be better. To be amongst the best that I can provide, like other authors on this
site that genuinely blow my mind with how brilliantly their stories are written.

Nowadays, I'm kind of writing to reach the scenes I wish to write about. I don't want to
let you down, and I don't wanna let myself down. I am grateful for each and every one
of you who continues to read my stories despite my abysmal upload schedule, and I
hope you can forgive the shorter chapter. I don't know if I'll do shorter chapters in the
future like this, but if it helps speed me up...

In any case, thank you for reading, and I hope you all have a great day.



Please drop by the Archive and comment to let the creator know if you enjoyed their work!
